# S22+ FYG8 Campaign Ledger

Append-only. One line per experiment action or material health/recovery state
transition. Rows through 2026-08-03T20:46:02Z include the historical Interim
Fast-Loop interval; later rows use the ordinary live authority in `AGENTS.md`
and the target contract. This replaces per-run prose reports for routine work
only. It does not replace the private structured result, append-only journal,
raw logs, or transfer accounting required by the selected tier and target
contract.

The trial retirement calculation is closed: the first `CAMPAIGN_CLOSED` rows
for `s22plus-fyg8-p296` and `s22plus-fyg8-p298` retired it at
2026-08-03T20:46:02Z. Duplicate close, parked, and per-action health rows never
alter that historical result.

Write a separate report only for a new capability, a new hazard class, an
incident, or a genuinely ambiguous device-safety result.

Metrics:

- conclusive experiment results per week: `PROVED + REFUTED`;
- conclusive information yield: `(PROVED + REFUTED) / all device attempts`;
- diagnostic-bearing yield:
  `(PROVED + REFUTED + NO_PROOF_EXPERIMENT_PRECONDITION) / all device attempts`;
- experiment-precondition no-proof rate:
  `NO_PROOF_EXPERIMENT_PRECONDITION / all device attempts`; and
- observer no-proof rate: `NO_PROOF_OBSERVER / all device attempts`.

An append-only proof-class correction never edits or removes the original
action row, terminal spelling, transfer count, health, or raw evidence. A later
H0 correction row must identify the original campaign/ordinal, name its
effective class, and cite the source-backed distinction. Metrics use that
effective class once; the correction row itself is not another device attempt.

Device safety is recorded independently from experiment proof. A timeout or
late endpoint may be `HEALTH_PENDING`, `HOST_OBSERVER_FAILURE`, or
`RECOVERY_PENDING_PARKED` without closing the campaign.

## Orthogonal taxonomy

The immutable proof column is a row-local evidence outcome. A normalized F1
attempt enters metric denominators when its maximum candidate-transfer count
reaches one, whether or not it later closes. Until one `CAMPAIGN_CLOSED` row
supplies experiment proof it is `ATTEMPT_OPEN`, not omitted. Intermediate
F1/recovery rows, pre-session stops, connected reads and controls, H0
capability work, reviews, analyses, and corrections do not independently add
attempts or experiment-proof outcomes. Device health, transfer-phase row kind,
experiment proof, and capability-review state are separate axes; H0 row kind
does not consume the review-state classifier.

For H0 capability rows, an action beginning `PASS_GO_` has review state
`PASS_GO`; an action ending `_REVIEW_PENDING` or `_PENDING_REVIEW` has review
state `IMPLEMENTED_REVIEW_PENDING`. Historical actions that merely contain
`REVIEW` have `LEGACY_UNSCOPED_REVIEW_LABEL`, not an inferred approval. Other
rows have `NOT_APPLICABLE`. A row cannot satisfy more than one state rule.
Raw review-state label counts are historical labels, not outstanding review
debt. Review obligations are keyed by `(campaign, review-topic)`, so unrelated
topics may remain open in parallel. A future resolution must use
`h0-<same-topic>-review-N`; six frozen nonconforming historical pairs resolve
only through exact identity mappings. An unrelated `PASS_GO_` never resolves
an obligation, and a second pending row is rejected only for the same topic.
Except for those six legacy pairs, a pending ordinal is `h0-<topic>-N` or
`h0-<topic>-followup-N`, with nonempty lowercase-alphanumeric topic segments
joined by single hyphens, no `review` segment, and a positive canonical `N`.

The format below governs new rows. Four frozen legacy rows omit only the
transfer cell; their findings prove zero transfers, so the auditor recognizes
those exact row identities as `0/0` and rejects every other omission. Legacy
`NO_PROOF`, `NO_PROOF_SUBTYPE`, and `NO_PROOF_LOG_BASELINE` spellings remain
row-local evidence qualifiers and never become experiment-proof classes.

Rows use a closed S22 campaign token, lowercase/hyphen ordinal, and uppercase
action token; timestamps are real nondecreasing UTC values and each
`(campaign, ordinal, action)` is unique. `PASS_GO_` and pending-review action
forms are reserved to H0. Non-F1 rows require `0/0`; F1 counts are one-shot,
rollback cannot precede candidate, and every candidate-bearing F1 ordinal is a
positive canonical integer with an optional `-recovery-close` suffix.
`CAMPAIGN_CLOSED` is F1-only with one candidate; a healthy close requires one
rollback, while an exhausted `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` close may retain 1/0 or 1/1.
Rows sharing one campaign and normalized attempt ordinal have componentwise
nondecreasing transfer counts, at most one terminal, and no F1 continuation
after that terminal; a `CLOSED` attempt has exactly one terminal. A
`-recovery-close` row requires the earlier base attempt.
The row schema has no candidate artifact identity. It audits attempt-local
one-shot counts but cannot prove that one candidate artifact was not reused
under another campaign or attempt ordinal; replay identity remains governed by
the higher-precedence Process-v2 evidence.

The following registry is the machine-readable authority for correction
scope. `CAMPAIGN_PROOF` changes the named attempt's effective class once for
metrics. `SUBRESULT_ONLY` preserves a narrower interpretation and can never
reclassify the campaign terminal.

`CORRECTION | s22plus-fyg8-p316 | 1 | s22plus-fyg8-p317 | h0-design-1 | CAMPAIGN_PROOF | experiment | NO_PROOF_EXPERIMENT_PRECONDITION | APPLY_TO_METRICS`

`CORRECTION | s22plus-fyg8-p317 | 1 | s22plus-fyg8-p317 | h0-endpoint-topology-correction-2 | SUBRESULT_ONLY | endpoint-selection | NO_PROOF_EXPERIMENT_PRECONDITION | EXCLUDE_FROM_CAMPAIGN_METRICS`

`CORRECTION | s22plus-fyg8-p318 | 1 | s22plus-fyg8-p318 | h0-postlive-eud-index-14 | CAMPAIGN_PROOF | pre-max77705-eud-index | NO_PROOF_EXPERIMENT_PRECONDITION | APPLY_TO_METRICS`

## Format

`<UTC> | <campaign> | <ordinal> | <tier> | <action> | <HEALTHY|HEALTH_PENDING|HOST_OBSERVER_FAILURE|RECOVERY_PENDING_PARKED|RECOVERY_REQUIRED> | <PROVED|REFUTED|NO_PROOF_EXPERIMENT_PRECONDITION|NO_PROOF_OBSERVER|N/A> | <candidate-transfers>/<rollback-transfers> | <one-line finding>`

2026-08-18T12:29:45Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | stage-a-probe-1 | D0 | P319_STAGE_A_PROBE_REGMAP_ABSENT_READ_ONLY | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | A read-only probe answered the Stage A question that Stage A itself could not, and located the Stage A defect that blocked it. There is no regmap entry under the Max77705 57-0066 I2C client, proved twice in one read by a complete ls -a listing and by a direct inode test, with reached_end true, lsa_rc 0, listing_count 15, and uevent present as the independent completeness check. The client holds max77705-charger, max77705-fuelgauge and max77705-usbc children, the standard driver, modalias, name, of_node, power, subsystem, uevent and wakeup nodes, a device-specific fw_update, and one device link named supplier:platform:c42d000.qcom,spmi:qcom,pm8350c@2:pinctrl@8800. That device link is the Stage A defect: its entry-name allowlist is A-Za-z0-9._:+- so commas and @ are rejected, the link sorts immediately after subsystem, and Stage A exited 23 there on a legitimate sysfs name, losing uevent, wakeup and all three trailing rows. The stop looked like success because exec-out su -c did not surface the remote exit 23 and adb reported returncode 0 with empty stderr and no truncation flag. Three hypotheses were refuted before the cause was found: script POSIX logic passes on dash, sh and busybox; transport loss is excluded because two other captures in the same run completed; and argv flattening is excluded because the predecessor s22plus_fyg8_max77705_sysfs_d0.py sends a 62-line 2046-byte script the same unquoted way and returned PASS on 2026-08-11. Stage A's parser was right to refuse a listing it could not prove complete, since that truncated listing also lacked uevent and would otherwise have been reported as regmap absent by luck rather than by evidence. Stage A is deliberately not repaired here because the fix changes its pinned safety digest e60e7104 and would only re-derive a result already held under stricter evidence. Registering the probe moved the raw-first auditor's closed-observer population from 121 to 122 and the auditor stopped until it was declared, which is the boundary behaving as designed. Stage B loses its assumed access path and its target must be re-derived from the three MFD children before any further device request; this row grants no Stage B authority. Probe script digest c63bb17d, 875 bytes, machine-checked as listing and inode tests only with zero sysfs writes, attribute-body reads, debugfs access, /dev/i2c access, module actions and reboots. device_writes false, reboot_requested false, partition_transfer false, candidate_used false, f1_authorized false, live_authorized false. 2026-08-18T12:35:42Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-stage-b-rederivation-1 | H0 | P319_STAGE_B_HAS_NO_SYSFS_READ_PATH_IMPLEMENTED_REVIEW_PENDING | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | Host-only re-derivation of the Stage B target from the candidate's materialized sources closes the sysfs branch with a negative result, and corrects a false claim this campaign published hours earlier. The corrected claim: CONTROL1 is not a sysfs attribute but an I2C register reached through the MUIC command protocol, where CONTROL1_R and CONTROL1_W are opcodes 0x05 and 0x06; the 2026-08-17 predecessor stop report already stated the real position, that whether Stage B has one exact regular attribute target was itself unproved. Two findings close the branch. First, the search location was wrong from the start: the MUIC attribute group is created on switch_device->kobj at drivers/usb/typec/maxim/max77705-muic.c:2553, not on the I2C client, so nothing that driver exposes could ever appear under 57-0066 and the absent regmap entry was not the loss of an assumed path. Second, CONTROL1 occurs exactly once in the entire MUIC driver, at :343, as COMMAND_CONTROL1_WRITE; no read opcode is issued anywhere in it and therefore none is reachable from sysfs. The exposed group splits three ways by what a read would prove: usb_sel returns pdata->usb_path and usb_state and attached_dev return muic_data->attached_dev, all software caches that cannot distinguish the mux being set from the driver believing it set the mux, which is exactly the question P3.15 left open; adc, vbus_value and vbus_value_pd perform a real single-register I2C read of USBC_STATUS1 at :591 and :608, which is safe and genuine but is a different register from CONTROL1 and does not answer the mux question; uart_sel, uart_en, otg_test, apo_factory, afc_disable and hiccup are writable and out of scope. Consequently Stage B cannot be a sysfs read at all, and reaching CONTROL1 requires the bound-diagnostic command-protocol path already described in the 2026-08-11 control-plane feasibility report, which is a materially larger step than a read. No Stage B authority is created and no device action is requested. This unit executed no device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live, A90 or S20+ action; all evidence is static source under kernel_platform/msm-kernel.

The rule is now a process-spawn-capability test over imports, call names and call attributes that also fails closed on unparseable source, and the transport gate additionally matches parser-folded string constants because a text regex could not see the literal "a" "d" "b". Nine of the ten are now rejected under any filename and are pinned as an executable corpus of eighteen cases in the test suite; the tenth, delegation of acquisition to a helper module outside the audited directory, is an accepted and stated residual because it needs interprocedural analysis, and is bounded by the fact that a helper inside revalidation is itself flagged. The S22 scope regex was anchored so that build_s22plus and s22_ sources, including those that write partitions, were held by name only and could be edited to add new device acquisition without failing the audit; they are now byte-frozen, moving the frozen population from 79 to 127 of 177 members, and a mutation appending a heimdall flash call to a candidate builder is now rejected. This campaign's own probe was measured and its acquisition test returned False, so the behavior rule never examined it and the filename rule stopped the audit, which contradicts the prior commit's claim that the boundary behaved as designed; the report now says so. The probe also defined STOP_VERDICT and never used it, so a timed-out or truncated capture would have rendered as PASS with an empty listing; the verdict is now gated on reached_end together with the capture handle's timed_out, output_exceeded and producer_error_type, which are recorded rather than dropped. The probe's safety contract is restated in the report as a lint rather than a proof, because nine of ten dangerous scripts pass it, including a sysfs write addressed through a shell variable, which is the idiom the probe itself uses. Auditor self binding, legacy observer inventory at 47, closed observer inventory at 122 and the pre-boundary inventory were all recomputed, and the receipt was regenerated as 20260818-04-failclosed-acquisition with predecessors preserved. Completeness is independently checked with reached_end true, lsa_rc 0, uevent_present yes, regmap_present no, timed_out false, output_exceeded false, producer_error_type null and 486 stdout bytes. This confirms by observation the source and config claim of the preceding unit, that the shipped kernel exposes the MAX77705 command protocol to userspace through max77705_debug.c, and it therefore stands as the direct refutation of this campaign's earlier closure that reaching CONTROL1 required a bound diagnostic module. What a reg read would cost was derived from the driver's table rather than from the attribute name: mxim_debug_reg_show walks a fixed seventeen-entry table at max77705_debug.c:32-117 and skips every entry marked ignore, which is exactly the three read-to-clear interrupt registers USBC_IRQ 0x02, CC_IRQ 0x03 and PD_IRQ 0x04, so a read performs fourteen single-byte i2c_smbus_read_byte_data calls over 0x00 to 0x10 covering UIC_HW_REV, UIC_FW_REV, RSVD1, USBC_STATUS1, USBC_STATUS2, BC_STATUS, RSVD2, CC_STATUS1, CC_STATUS2, PD_STATUS1, PD_STATUS2 and the three interrupt masks. Neither the charger firmware-major register nor any charger detail register is in that table and the charger and fuelgauge blocks are separate I2C children, so the standing hazard that motivated the full regmap dump prohibition is not on this path; the prohibition itself is unchanged and this is not a dump. The show buffer was checked rather than assumed, since dump is 216 bytes against a header of ten and fourteen rows of eleven for a total of 164. Stage B consequently revives as a partial read and not as a CONTROL1 read, and this row states that limit rather than repeating the campaign's two previous wrong Stage B claims: CC_STATUS1, CC_STATUS2, PD_STATUS1, PD_STATUS2 and BC_STATUS are exposed nowhere in the MUIC sysfs group so they are genuine new state, while CONTROL1 is at no address the table covers and the COMN1SW and COMP2SW mux fields stay unreachable by reading, because reaching them requires issuing opcode CONTROL1_R 0x05 by writing AP_DATAOUT, which is a command to the PD controller and not a D0. Three new write hazards are recorded and not exercised: both debug0 attributes are mode 0664 at :232 and :286 so root may write either, mxim_debug_reg_store performs an arbitrary single-register I2C write with no address validation at :209-230, mxim_debug_opcode_store writes thirty-three bytes to 0x21 through 0x41 and so issues an arbitrary opcode command at :255-284, and the /dev/mxim_dev ioctls MXIM_DEBUG_REG_WRITE and MXIM_DEBUG_OPCODE_WRITE are the same primitives without bounds at :350-360 and :314-333; these join fw_update as surfaces that must not be written without F1-class authority and none of them has been written. A separate concurrency hazard is recorded: the ioctl handler takes mxim_pdev->lock but neither sysfs show nor sysfs store takes any lock and none of the three participates in the opcode queue max77705_usbc.c maintains for driver traffic, so a debug opcode write races an in-flight driver opcode with no arbitration on either side, which is a reason to defer even the opcode read rather than only to forbid the write. This unit also reports a defect in its own probe: ls -a on the two mxim directories returned column-collapsed so mxim_class_nodes and mxim_debug0_entries are each a single element while the 57-0066 listing in the same run returned one entry per line, the difference being entry-name width alone; the conclusion is unaffected because the bracket-e and bracket-d scalar rows answer existence independently of listing shape, but the parser is width-dependent, which is the same class of defect as the Stage A truncation this probe exists to diagnose, and any successor must use ls -a1 and must not derive a count from a row that could be a column. Probe safety contract machine-checked as listing and inode tests only with zero sysfs writes, attribute-body reads, debugfs access, /dev/i2c access, module actions and reboots; fw_update, reg and opcode were neither read nor written and only their names were listed. device_writes false, reboot_requested false, partition_transfer false, candidate_used false, f1_authorized false, live_authorized false. The A90 attached in recovery on the same host received no command and no S20+ action occurred.

The preceding row said the driver skips the read-to-clear interrupt registers and left the impression that all of them are skipped; three of four are. Severity is stated rather than softened: this is not a partition, not firmware and not a brick, it is one dropped alternate-mode interrupt in the worst case and recoverable by replug, but it is a state change on a path this campaign called a read. The same mislabelling runs through the rest of the table and would corrupt any decoder built from the debug header, so the register map was taken from what the driver actually reads: 0x09 is REG_UIC_FW_MINOR, 0x0A and 0x0B are REG_CC_STATUS0 and REG_CC_STATUS1, 0x0C and 0x0D are REG_PD_STATUS0 and REG_PD_STATUS1, and 0x0E is REG_UIC_INT_M. max77705.h is itself inconsistent because its CC bitfield comment blocks are labelled one higher than its address defines, so the CC layout was resolved from max77705_cc.c:340, :473, :535 and :584 reading CCPinStat, CCIStat, CCVcnStat and CCStat out of cc_status0 and :342 and :344 reading VSAFE0V and ConnStat out of cc_status1, while the PD side needed no correction because max77705_pd.c:1483, :1503, :1579 and :1688 match the header labels. The verdict is gated rather than asserted on the end sentinel, target presence, a zero cat status, the header row, an address set exactly equal to the fourteen expected, zero unparsed rows, a non-all-zero dump, a non-zero identity register, and the capture handle's timed_out, output_exceeded and producer_error_type; the all-zero refusal exists because mxim_debug_i2c_read assigns its int return into an unsigned char so a failed read is not distinguishable from a real zero by value. Registering the runner moved the closed-observer population from 122 to 123 and the auditor stopped until it was declared; the file was deliberately named so OBSERVER_FILE_RE would catch it, which is a workaround for the residual the boundary review already recorded and not a fix for it. Sixteen focused tests pass and the raw-first audit returns PASS. This row implements a runner only and creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay, device or live authority; opcode, fw_update and /dev/mxim_dev remain F1-class and untouched and full regmap dumps remain forbidden. The values are UIC_HW_REV 0x1a, UIC_FW_REV 0x6e, VDM_INT 0x00, USBC_STATUS1 0x27, USBC_STATUS2 0x05, BC_STATUS 0x82, UIC_FW_MINOR 0x40, CC_STATUS0 0xa1, CC_STATUS1 0x09, PD_STATUS0 0x19, PD_STATUS1 0x47, UIC_INT_M 0x04, CC_INT_M 0x20 and PD_INT_M 0x00. The recorded read side effect cost nothing on this run because REG_VDM_INT read back 0x00, so no alternate-mode interrupt was latched and none was consumed; that is a fact about this run and not a reason to drop the gate, since the same read at a different moment would have taken whatever was pending. What is established: the controller is alive and normally initialised because all three interrupt masks sit at their compiled-in initial values REG_UIC_INT_M_INIT 0x04, REG_CC_INT_M_INIT 0x20 and REG_PD_INT_M_INIT 0x00 at max77705.h:95-98, so probe ran and nothing has since rewritten them; and the port state is unambiguous with the cable on CC2, the port a sink, the source advertising 1.5A through Rp, VBUS present at 4.5 to 5.5V, the ID line open so there is no factory jig and no OTG ground, BC1.2 classifying the far end as CDP, and no PD contract because PSRDY is 0 and the data role is UFP. Three values are recorded with care rather than as headlines. PDMsg and SYSMsg are last-event registers and not live state: SYSMsg reads SYSMSG_BOOT_POR so the USBC MCU has not been reset since power-on, and PDMsg reads HARDRESET_SENT so at some point since that power-on the controller sent a PD hard reset, with the timing not recoverable from this read. VSAFE0V is 1 while VBUSDet is 1 and VBADC reports 4.5 to 5.5V, which contradict as live levels; the reading most consistent with HARDRESET_SENT is that the bit is latched from the hard-reset sequence that drives VBUS to vSafe0V and has not been cleared, and this is explicitly recorded as inference and not measurement because the driver only samples the bit inside an interrupt handler at max77705_cc.c:342 and nothing in the sources states its clear semantics. Altmode is 1 while PD_ENTER_MODE is 0 and VDM_INT is 0, and no claim that an alternate mode is active is made from it. The mux question is not answered, because CONTROL1 is at no address in this table and COMN1SW and COMP2SW are unread, exactly as the preceding unit predicted. One constraint follows and is stated at its real strength: BC1.2 returning CHGTYP_CDP rather than CHGTYP_NO_VOLTAGE means the D+/D- handshake completed so those lines reach the charger-detection block electrically, but whether that block sits before or after the MUIC switch matrix is not established by anything read here, so this constrains the reading that D+/D- were never connected at all without resolving where the switch sits. Decoding tables for UIDADC, ChgTyp, CCIStat, SYSMsg and PDMsg were added to the runner after the read and are host-side only, leaving the remote script digest unchanged at 17755bf5 and 290 bytes, and the observed dump is pinned in the suite so the decoder cannot drift from the measurement. 2026-08-18T20:29:22Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-auditor-stale-bytecode-1 | H0 | P319_AUDITOR_STALE_BYTECODE_CONSTANTS_CLOSED_REVIEW_PENDING | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The raw-first auditor's own self-binding test failed and the cause was a stale bytecode hole in the auditor rather than a hole in its rule. test_loaded_auditor_cannot_receipt_different_source_bytes builds a mutation by replacing the auditor's pinned AUDITOR_NORMALIZED_SHA256 with zeros and expects a refusal, and no refusal came. Registering a new observer replaces one 64-hex digest with another so the file keeps exactly the same size, and when both the inventory edit and the self-binding edit land inside a single mtime second CPython's pyc invalidation check, which is mtime plus size, does not trip; the imported module therefore carried its constants from stale bytecode while the file on disk held different values, so the test built its mutation from the stale constant, found no occurrence of it in the source and mutated nothing. The audits themselves were not affected because audit_sources delegates to load_bound_auditor which compiles from the file, so every receipt this campaign published was produced from on-disk source; what was wrong is that the module could report one set of constants while auditing under another. Deleting the stale pyc fixes only the symptom, so the hole is closed instead: audit_sources now parses the digest literal out of its own source and refuses with executing auditor constants differ from its source when it differs from the executing constant, and a test sets the module constant to zeros, asserts the refusal and then asserts the unmutated module still audits. The failure mode is quiet and general, since any byte-pinned constant rotated in place at the same length within one second can leave a Python process auditing under constants its own file no longer contains. The auditor self-binding and the receipt were regenerated after the fix and the full auditor suite passes at sixteen tests. This row changes a boundary and therefore carries a review obligation. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred.
2026-08-18T20:31:25Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | log-harvest-2 | D0 | P319_STOCK_MUX_COMMAND_POSITIVE_CONTROL_READ_ONLY | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | A cable replug placed an attach inside the ring window and the harvest returned conclusive, establishing a byte-level positive control for the mux question without a single write. mux_evidence_conclusive is true with a 39.767 second ring span, 733 driver lines, 9 attach markers, 2 switch_path calls, 6 opcode write dumps and 11 opcode messages. The captured sequence is complete on both edges: at detach, CCPINSTAT NO_DETERMINATION then PLUG_DETACHED then com_to_open then max77705_switch_path value(0x3f) then i2c_opcode_write opcode 0x6 write_length 2 then the wire dump opcode_write 06 3f; and at attach, CCPINSTAT CC2_ACTIVE then PLUG_ATTACHED then ccstat cc_SINK then max77705_muic_attach_usb_path usb_path=0 then com_to_usb_ap then max77705_switch_path value(0x9) then i2c_opcode_write opcode 0x6 write_length 2 then the wire dump opcode_write 06 09. Both values decode exactly from max77705-muic.h:384-393 with the arithmetic recomputed from the header shifts rather than assumed: COM_OPEN is NOBCCOMP 0, RCPS 0, COMP2SW 0x7 open and COMN1SW 0x7 open giving 0x3f, and COM_USB is NOBCCOMP 0, RCPS 0, COMP2SW 0x1 USB and COMN1SW 0x1 USB giving 0x09. Opcode 0x06 is COMMAND_CONTROL1_WRITE and the window contains exactly one 06 3f and exactly one 06 09 and no other CONTROL1 write, so the command was serialised and handed to I2C rather than merely computed in software. What is settled is that on the stock running unit the driver does command the D+/D- mux to USB on attach and that the command reaches the wire, answering whether the driver ever tried with byte-level evidence. What is not settled is whether the MUIC silicon applied it, because what is observed is the write leaving the AP and not the resulting switch position; reading the position back still requires issuing CONTROL1_R, which is a write and remains F1-class, so the gap has narrowed from was it commanded to was it applied and has not closed. This is a positive control and not a refutation of the campaign hypothesis, which concerns candidate boot images while this run is the stock unit; its value is that a candidate run can now be compared against exact expectations, namely com_to_usb_ap, max77705_switch_path value(0x9), and a wire dump of 06 09 within roughly a second of PLUG_ATTACHED, and if a candidate attach lacks 06 09 the hypothesis is confirmed at wire level and still without any write. Two earlier notes are refined rather than overturned: the fw_update hazard's CONTROL1 write of 0x09 is now identified as COM_USB rather than an arbitrary value, with the hazard unchanged because entering the firmware-update opcode path is the danger and not the mux value; and the debug opcode concurrency hazard is strengthened, because the queue-contention branch at max77705_usbc.c:2499 logged !!!current_cmd.opcode twice in forty seconds during entirely normal operation, which is exactly the contention an unsynchronised debug write would be injected into. The redactor reported zero redactions. No write, no opcode issued by this campaign, no ring clearing and no interrupt consumed; reg, opcode, fw_update and /dev/mxim_dev were untouched. device_writes false, ring_buffer_cleared false, reboot_requested false, partition_transfer false, candidate_used false, f1_authorized false, live_authorized false. The A90 attached in recovery on the same host received no command and no S20+ action occurred.
2026-08-18T20:41:04Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-usblog-parse-1 | H0 | P319_USBLOG_RINGS_SPAN_BOOT_IMPLEMENTED_REVIEW_PENDING | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The /proc/usblog capture already held was parsed structurally and it changes which surface the candidate comparison should use. Unlike the kernel ring, which spanned 39.767 seconds in the conclusive run, usblog is a set of named rings bounded by entry count rather than by time, and every count sits far below its cap: CCIC EVENT 40 of 512, USB STATE 55 of 256, USB EVENT 10 of 64, USB_MODE 5 of 64, EXTRA 4 of 128, PORT 0 of 128 and PCM 0 of 64, with no ring wrapped and every declared count equal to the number of entries the parser actually recovered so nothing was dropped either by overwrite or by parsing. The earliest entry is at 3.605282 and the latest at 179808.320553, so spans_boot is true across roughly 49.9 hours, and the header carries a time sync line mapping monotonic 179813.278371 to wall clock 08-18 20:31:24. The parse discharges an open note rather than only adding data: Stage B read PDMsg HARDRESET_SENT and this campaign recorded it as a last-event register whose timing could not be recovered, and the EXTRA ring recovers it as PDIC HARDRESET_SENT at 4.215202 and at 179808.319417, one per attach, so a PD hard reset is sent on every attach here and Stage B's reading was routine rather than anomalous; this also supports the VSAFE0V latched inference because the attach hard reset precedes the sampled window by about half a second. The CCIC ring independently cross-checks the mux evidence, with ID_CONNECT ATTACHED at 179807.771036 and ID_USB ATTACH_UFP at 179807.774297 against the kernel log's com_to_usb_ap and switch_path value(0x9) at 179807.846, two rings agreeing on one attach within 75 milliseconds. Controller identity is recorded as read without interpretation: hw version 0 0 0 1a matching Stage B's UIC_HW_REV 0x1a, sw version 0 40 6e 0 matching UIC_FW_REV 0x6e and UIC_FW_MINOR 0x40, and bin version 15 40 6e 0 as the image the kernel carries, with two of four fields identical and the first differing and no claim made about whether an update is pending because the field semantics are not established from source. Gadget enumeration is also retained, with CONNDONE 17, RESET SUPER 15, GET_DES 6 and SET_CON 5 across the uptime and the replug producing VBUS_SESSION_EN then CONNDONE SS then RESET SUPER then GET_DES then SET_CON, so whether enumeration completed is persistently recorded without any write. The consequence for the campaign is that a candidate comparison needs no live capture and no write: the interesting candidate events happen at boot where the kernel ring will have rotated, these rings will not have, and CONFIG_PSTORE_CONSOLE and CONFIG_PSTORE_RAM are both set at waipio-gki_defconfig:581-584 so a candidate that never brings up ADB can still be read from the previous boot's console after rebooting to stock. Five parser tests are added covering ring capacity and wrap detection, version and attach and hard-reset extraction, empty input, and end-to-end carriage in the harvest observation, and sixteen focused tests pass on the runner with 113 across the campaign's suites. This unit also records an operator error: while re-registering the changed harvester an unanchored sed ran with an empty capture variable, rewriting the first empty string literal in the auditor and leaving it syntactically invalid, and a second blanked a digest pin in a test; both were restored, the auditor from HEAD and the pin by hand, and the full diff was reviewed to confirm only the intended line changed, and registration edits must henceforth use anchored patterns with a non-empty check because they are byte-pinned digests. No device action occurred in this unit; the analysis is of evidence already captured. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred.
2026-08-18T20:57:54Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | log-harvest-3 | D0 | P319_PSTORE_EMPTY_LAST_KMSG_IS_SEC_LOG_BUF_READ_ONLY | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | A read-only probe tested this campaign's own recommendation and refuted it, and the repository already held the answer. The preceding unit recommended pstore as the retained-console fallback for a candidate that never brings up ADB, resting on CONFIG_PSTORE_CONSOLE and CONFIG_PSTORE_RAM being set. Measured on the running unit, /sys/fs/pstore is mounted and contains zero entries, so a config symbol being set is not a retained log; and this was already recorded, because the 2026-07-07 M10A3 live result states pstore files none and /proc/last_kmsg bytes 2097136, the same two facts and the same byte count six weeks earlier, so the recommendation was made without checking this repository's own live evidence. The surface is also not what was named: /proc/last_kmsg here is Samsung sec_log_buf at drivers/samsung/debug/log_buf/sec_log_buf_last_kmsg.c, where __last_kmsg_pull_last_log copies the previous boot's log region into a vmalloc buffer of ___log_buf_get_buf_size() at probe and serves it through proc, so it is the previous boot's kernel log ring snapshot at this boot rather than a pstore file or a ramoops record. That model predicts the bounded head read, which returned 36 lines with no Linux version banner and an earliest timestamp of 39393.729171, giving starts_at_a_boot false: a circular buffer whose head sits wherever the ring wrapped, about 10.9 hours into the previous session. The 2097136 figure is the buffer size and reads identically whatever the buffer holds, which is why size alone was refused as evidence and a bounded 4096-byte head was read instead, never the whole 2 MiB. The consequence cuts both ways, because a long session wraps and loses its own early boot while a candidate that fails early emits little and would fit without wrapping, and that is the case that matters and the case this stock measurement cannot confirm. The 2026-07-08 M18 live result bears directly on it, recording that the capture did not find the expected marker in pstore or retained last-kmsg and that the retained log looks more like ABL or download-mode retention than the candidate printk stream, leaving two open questions and one instruction to analyze the private 2 MiB last_kmsg, an instruction never carried out. Checklist item three is therefore not proven usable for the candidate failure mode and is not refuted either; it is untested in the only way that would settle it. The next unit is free of device cost, because candidate-last_kmsg.bin from the 2026-07-10 v3437 run and first-stock-boot-last_kmsg.bin from v3439 both exist on disk at 2097136 bytes each, so deciding whether the retained-console path can carry a candidate's early boot is a host-only analysis of evidence already captured. Three corrections are carried: the named path was wrong since pstore is empty and the surface is /proc/last_kmsg, the mechanism was wrong since it is sec_log_buf rather than ramoops or pstore, and the claim that a candidate can still be read from the previous boot's console is downgraded from stated capability to untested hypothesis. The probe reads listings and bounded heads only, never the 2 MiB body, and the redactor reported zero redactions. Stock loads common_muic.ko with muic_param_pdic_info=1 muic_param_pmic_info=3 muic_param_afc_mode=0x30, there is no modules.options anywhere in vendor_dlkm, vendor or the vendor_boot ramdisk, the source is the kernel command line carrying common_muic.muic_param_pmic_info=3 which Android's libmodprobe converts to module arguments, and insmod does not do this while the kernel applies modname.param= only to built-in code, so a candidate inserting modules directly supplies none of them; that looked decisive because get_switch_sel returns pmic_info & 0xfff and bit 0 selects MUIC_PATH_USB_AP against MUIC_PATH_USB_CP so routing D+/D- to the modem is exactly the observed shape, but the shipped common_muic.ko carries no pmic_info, charging_mode or ccic_info symbol of any kind so the static int pmic_info = -1 branch is what was compiled, the fallback is -1 & 0xfff = 0xfff, bit 0 is set, and the path is AP, the same result as the stock value of 3; the identical guard and the absence of bare lpcharge and factory_mode symbols in pdic_notifier_module.ko disposes of pdic_param_lpcharge the same way. muic-core.c calls pdata->set_gpio_usb_sel in two places which would be a second physical switching element outside the MAX77705, and set_gpio_usb_sel is never assigned anywhere in the tree so both calls are skipped. usb_notify_sysfs.c:1260 sets udev->usb_data_enabled = 1 at device creation so that gate does not need userspace to open it. mode_store refuses peripheral when is_blocked reports NOTIFY_BLOCK_TYPE_CLIENT, which would be a silent userspace-shaped veto on the role request, and is_blocked returns false on a NULL otg_notify and again on a NULL u_notify so a candidate that has not brought up the notifier is not blocked by it. It reads eight attributes and writes nothing: a600000.ssusb/mode, the UDC's state, function, current_speed, maximum_speed, is_a_peripheral and is_selfpowered, and the configfs g1/UDC binding, plus a listing of /sys/class/udc so the controller name is observed rather than assumed even though init.target.rc:130 and the vendor_boot bootconfig both name a600000.dwc3. Every one is a DEVICE_ATTR_RO whose show function is a plain struct-field read, so no I2C is issued and no latched interrupt is consumed, which makes this strictly weaker than the Stage B register read already run and is why it carries no acknowledgement flag. What mode means is stated precisely rather than by its name: mode_show calls dwc3_msm_get_role which returns DEVICE when mdwc->vbus_active is set and HOST when mdwc->id_state is DWC3_ID_GROUND, and those are exactly the two fields dwc3_msm_set_role assigns, so mode is a faithful readback of the role the driver has been told to take and is not evidence that the controller negotiated anything; the preceding report's phrase the controller's actual current role was imprecise on this point and this row is the correction. The second half comes from the gadget core, where state_show returns usb_state_string(gadget->state) which advances only as a host drives enumeration, so the runner classifies into role_not_peripheral, role_only, bound_not_attached, attached_not_configured and configured, which is the split the frontier needed. orientation is deliberately not read because orientation_show reports mdwc->orientation_override, a debug override rather than the CC orientation. The safety contract is equality rather than a token lint: the remote script is rendered from the read table and the contract requires the script to equal that rendering, so widening what the device does requires widening a reviewable table, and a reordered script with the same paths is still refused; the suite exercises an added read, an added redirect, and the pull-up primitives soft_connect and srp by name. Two defects were found by that machinery during construction and both are recorded rather than smoothed. The contract failed closed on its author's own script because the forbidden-token list contained the configfs UDC path, which matched the existence test on the same path; the token was removed because a write there is already excluded structurally by redirect_count zero and by the rendering equality, and forbidding a path the script must legitimately test was the wrong instrument. More seriously, the suite caught that collect() contract-checked the module-level script through a default argument bound once at definition time while sending the value looked up at call time, so the checked text and the executed text could diverge; the script is now resolved once into a local that is both checked and sent, the contract's parameter defaults to None and resolves at call time, and a test rebinds the module attribute to prove it. A third correction was to the transcript format itself: the status line was emitted inside the delimited body, making an attribute value and its status ambiguous, so the status is now captured before the body closes and reported after it, the parser claims body content before considering any marker, and a test feeds an attribute whose value is literally shaped like a status line. Twenty-two tests cover the contract, the parser, the classifier across all five stages and the refusal to create a run directory when the contract fails. The runner joins the closed raw-first observer inventory at 126 sources and the pre-boundary device source inventory is unchanged, so it was classified as a compliant observer rather than a legacy acquiring source; the audit receipt for this unit is raw-first-observer-audit-20260819-01-usb-role-state.json at 10296 bytes with SHA-256 5cd4258d8cb1ddb01af5c6b96855a8cc2b6e0d979e02187da6845d133e6fcfd4, and the superseded log-harvest receipt is preserved unmodified with its own test because its filename names that unit and rewriting it would falsify that unit's evidence. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred, and this row creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay or live authority.

First, this is not a new control: the 2026-07-26 P278 three-lane analysis already records the exact stock recipe as vendor.usb.use_gadget_hal=0, UDC a600000.dwc3, parent mode peripheral, UDC state configured and UDC speed super-speed, so five of the nine values were already held, and what this unit adds is that the tuple is unchanged three and a half weeks and several boots later, that it now comes from a contract-bound raw-first runner instead of an ad-hoc capture, and four values that recipe did not carry, being function, maximum_speed, is_a_peripheral and is_selfpowered. Mounting the filesystem read-only removes the obstacle because the host kernel decompresses on read, and the answer is that the two files are not merely the same build but the same bytes, both 423456 bytes with SHA-256 27e988788242888dc0c3acaf835a66585c024b034b07741e619b674ee77db3db. Comparing every module rather than the one that was asked about turns the fact into a structural property: the vendor_boot ramdisk holds 441 ko files and vendor_dlkm holds 356, 306 names appear in both, and all 306 are byte identical with zero differences. The two disjoint sets are not arbitrary either. 135 modules are ramdisk-only and every one of the 135 is in the ramdisk's own 140-entry first-stage modules.load, so the first stage's exclusive modules are exactly the ones vendor_dlkm does not re-ship; 50 modules are vendor_dlkm-only and they are entirely late-boot media and data, being the lpass_cdc, wcd, swr and q6 audio stack, msm_video, msm-eva, camera and hdmi, and the rmnet and ipa networking modules, with not one of them matching usb, typec, muic, pdic, dwc, phy or redriver. Every CONTROL1 write in the MUIC driver funnels through max77705_switch_path, so enumerating its callers enumerates the writers, and there are eleven enclosing functions: max77705_muic_attach_usb_path writing COM_USB or COM_USB_CP by pdata->usb_path, max77705_muic_handle_attach the same, max77705_muic_handle_detach writing COM_OPEN twice with JIG and NONE unconditional and USB, CDP, OTG and TIMEOUT_OPEN only when ccic_evt_attached is MUIC_PDIC_NOTI_DETACH, max77705_muic_logically_detach writing COM_OPEN only when force_path_open, max77705_muic_detect_dev writing COM_OPEN, COM_USB_CP and COM_OPEN in its water branch, hiccup_store writing COM_OPEN on a userspace sysfs write, switch_to_ap_uart and switch_to_cp_uart writing the UART paths, max77705_muic_shutdown writing COM_OPEN, max77705_muic_set_pogo_adc writing COM_OPEN, and write_vps_regs. A method correction is recorded because the obvious instrument is wrong for this code: every one of these is static and the compiler inlines them, so readelf reports com_to_usb_ap, com_to_open and max77705_switch_path as absent from the shipped pdic_max77705.ko while the stock boot log prints max77705_switch_path value(0x9); the instrument that works is the __func__ string literal each pr_info carries, since an inlined static function still leaves its name in .rodata, and on that test all ten of the live writers plus write_vps_regs are present. Two of the eleven are not writers on this device. write_vps_regs is dead as a writer because its only max77705_switch_path call sits inside an #if 0, so the function still runs and logs but never writes CONTROL1 and there is no restore-previous-path behaviour. max77705_muic_set_pogo_adc is not compiled, being guarded by CONFIG_MUIC_SM5504_POGO whose __func__ string is absent from the shipped module as is max77705_muic_disable_chgdet from the nested CONFIG_MUIC_DISABLE_CHGDET block, so pogo cannot open the path here. The water branch by contrast is compiled including its reroute to CP: it is gated behind !is_lpcharge_pdic_param() && !is_factory_start and its com_to_usb_cp call additionally behind CONFIG_HICCUP_CHARGER, and that config is enabled because the exact string water hiccup mode, Aux USB path is present in the shipped module along with initialize hiccup state and device type at hiccup booting, so on water with VBUS present the driver actively moves the mux to the CP path rather than merely opening it, with afc_water_disable initialised false at probe and set true only by the PDIC water notification. The answer to the question asked is therefore yes: after a successful COM_USB the path can be reopened or moved, and four of the mechanisms need no userspace at all, being detach handling, logical detach with force_path_open, the water branch and shutdown, while only hiccup_store requires a userspace write which no candidate performs. What stock actually does is unusually clean and both retained 2026-07-10 captures agree: com_to_usb_ap appears once, max77705_switch_path appears once, and com_to_open, com_to_usb_cp, WATER DETECT, water hiccup mode and PDIC_NOTIFY_ID_WATER each appear zero times, so a healthy stock boot writes CONTROL1 exactly once to the AP USB path and nothing reopens it, bounded by the retained window that the last_kmsg unit measured at roughly 25 to 30 seconds so this is a statement about boot and early runtime rather than the whole session. Scope is stated rather than assumed: none of this graph runs on a P3.17 candidate because that plan omits the stock driver in favour of the diagnostic, and it matters instead for the candidates that did load pdic_max77705, being S7A2, M7, M11, M12 and M18, where the water branch is the one mechanism that could both fire without userspace and leave the mux pointing somewhere other than the AP; that is a hypothesis with a cheap test rather than a finding, because those runs would carry the WATER DETECT or Aux USB path strings in their logs and the campaign's record states they did not preserve the MUIC sequence at all, so the test cannot be run on them retrospectively. All inputs are the materialized candidate kernel sources, the shipped module read through a read-only loop mount, and captures already on this host. 2026-08-19T02:44:48Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-role-pullup-trace-1 | H0 | P319_ROLE_TO_PULLUP_TRACED_AND_BSESSVLD_IS_THE_GATE | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The review's fifth and last ranked item is closed, completing its host-only list. The chain from a mode write to a pull-up is mode_store mapping peripheral to USB_ROLE_DEVICE and calling dwc3_msm_set_role, which sets mdwc->vbus_active true and mdwc->id_state DWC3_ID_FLOAT and calls dwc3_ext_event_notify, which translates those fields into the mdwc->inputs bitmap as ID, B_SESS_VLD and B_SUSPEND and queues sm_work, after which dwc3_otg_sm_work in DRD_STATE_IDLE takes the peripheral branch only when ID and B_SESS_VLD are both set and calls dwc3_otg_start_peripheral, which notifies the redriver and both PHYs of connect and starts the gadget from which the core reaches usb_gadget_connect and dwc3_gadget_pullup. The load-bearing fact is that B_SESS_VLD is the gate and is not simply vbus_active: the bit is set from vbus_active only when hs_phy->flags & EUD_SPOOF_DISCONNECT is clear and is cleared instead when that flag is set, and the flag is sticky because once set every later notify with vbus_active true takes the clearing branch and only a transition to vbus_active false or an EUD connect removes it. That yields a mechanism for something this campaign has been treating as a preference: mode_show calls dwc3_msm_get_role which reports USB_ROLE_DEVICE whenever vbus_active is set, and dwc3_msm_set_role sets that field unconditionally before dwc3_ext_event_notify decides anything, so with EUD_SPOOF_DISCONNECT set a mode write succeeds and mode reads back peripheral while the state machine never leaves DRD_STATE_IDLE; the role readback and the gadget state can disagree and only reading both catches it, which is why the role/state runner reads mode together with the UDC state rather than mode alone. The extcon topology is pinned from the vendor_boot DTB rather than assumed: a600000.ssusb carries extcon = <0x139>, a single phandle, and 0x139 is qcom,msm-eud@88e0000, so every extcon event dwc3-msm can receive on this device is an EUD event, the notifier's eud_str branch always applies, and the plain vbus_active = event else-branch is unreachable here with vbus_active instead driven by the role switch that mode_store and the Type-C manager both use. One self-correction is recorded: a first reading of disable_eud stopped short of its end and concluded that disabling EUD leaves the extcon disconnected, which is wrong because disable_eud performs its spoof disconnect and then after the CSR write and a usleep_range issues extcon_set_state_sync(EXTCON_USB, true), and enable_eud likewise ends connected, so both settle with eud_active at 1 and their transient 1 to 0 is followed by a connect that sets EUD_SPOOF_CONNECT and restores B_SESS_VLD, making neither the hazard. The hazard is eud_event_notifier, which runs from the EUD hardware interrupt and on EUD_INT_VBUS sets EXTCON_JIG true before publishing usb_attach; in dwc3-msm's notifier the spoof variable is that JIG state and the early return that would divert a disconnect into dwc3_override_vbus_status is guarded by !spoof, so with spoof true the code falls through to check_eud_state true while eud_active is 0 and the next notify sets EUD_SPOOF_DISCONNECT and clears B_SESS_VLD with nothing to undo it. That path needs the EUD hardware interrupt and therefore EUD enabled in hardware, and whether it is enabled on this unit cannot be settled statically because msm_eud_hw_is_enabled reads a register and the probe path publishes an extcon connect only when the bootloader left EUD on; the retained stock captures show eud.ko loaded with empty args and eud in the module list while the one usb: eud_ser_upd line belongs to the bootloader tail rather than the kernel, so the cheap settling read is /sys/module/eud/parameters/enable at mode 0644. Finally one open question narrows rather than closes: dwc3_otg_start_peripheral calls vbus_session_notify(dwc->gadget, on, EAGAIN) under CONFIG_USB_CONFIGFS_F_SS_MON_GADGET, and that symbol is undefined in the shipped dwc3-msm.ko and defined in usb_f_ss_mon_gadget.ko, so the ss_mon module is a hard load-time dependency of dwc3-msm rather than optional telemetry, which is why modules.dep lists it and why the closure carries it; this does not answer whether the ss_mon.etc function instance that every stock composition links as f2 matters, because the notify happens on the gadget regardless of which functions are linked, and that narrower question stays open. All inputs are the materialized candidate kernel sources, the extracted vendor_boot DTB, shipped modules read through a read-only loop mount, and captures already on this host. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred, and this row creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay or live authority.

A test that pinned the old wording failed exactly as designed and was updated to pin the new fact instead of freezing a statement that had stopped being true, and it now also asserts the absence of both retired phrases. Two other suites fail on this host for reasons predating the edit, confirmed by re-running them with the change stashed: p318 docs fails on a frozen receipt reporting d0 adapter identity changed, and the kernel transfer manifest fails on a missing private toolchain directory. The second defect was structural rather than factual. The session report had been written by appending each unit's findings, which left What remains open at line 205 with five closing sections after it reached by strikethrough, put Evidence mid-document, and placed the frontier claim ahead of the section that withdrew it, so a reader going top to bottom met conclusions before their corrections. The fifteen sections are now ordered for reading rather than for discovery, with What remains open and Evidence last, the open list rewritten to carry only the four genuinely open items instead of a struck-through backlog, and every directional cross-reference checked so that all four remaining section above references point backwards and no section below reference survives. Four new tests pin the order itself, the last two sections, the absence of forward references and the absence of strikethrough in the open list, so the append-log shape cannot return silently; the report suite is 44 tests and the chronology it no longer carries is preserved by this ledger, which is append-only. 2026-08-19T03:05:27Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-bootloader-read-1 | H0 | P319_BOOTLOADER_EXTRACTED_AND_MUX_UNSUPPORTED_IN_WINDOW | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | A standing statement is withdrawn and the question behind it is narrowed. This campaign and the independent review both recorded that bootloader behaviour was undecidable because the extracted AP material contains no analysable BL, S-Boot, ABL or XBL image; that is true of the AP material and misleading as a conclusion, because the firmware ZIP already on this host has five members and one of them is BL_S906NKSS7FYG8_S906NKSS7FYG8_MQB99315260_REV00_user_low_ship_MULTI_CERT.tar.md5 at 114319472 bytes which had simply never been extracted. The bootloader was not missing, it was unread. It is now extracted to 30 images including uefi.elf, abl.elf, xbl_s.melf, xbl_config.elf, XblRamdump.elf, engmode.mbn, tz.mbn, devcfg.mbn and aop.mbn, with only NON-HLOS.bin and dspso.bin skipped as irrelevant bulk. One image is readable and it programs the MUIC: XblRamdump.elf is plaintext and carries Samsung MUIC bring-up as format strings, being %s : muic_init, %s : muic_init_hv_control and %s : muic_set_path to USB, together with EUD control as usb_eud_is_active status, usb_eud_is_active enable failed, usb_eud_is_active disable failed, qusb_dci_eud_init, qusb_dci_eud_recovery and the literal 0x88E0000 which is the same EUD register base the device tree names, so a bootloader stage does program the mux path to USB and a bootloader stage does manage EUD. Three images are excluded from that reading rather than cleared by it: muic_set_path appears in no other BL image, but measured over non-padding bytes uefi.elf has 7.97 bits per byte, abl.elf 7.99 and xbl_s.melf 7.32, effectively random, so those images are encrypted or compressed and a string search cannot see into them, which blocks static analysis of the UEFI and ABL stages rather than answering it. The retained captures settle more than the images do because both carry the bootloader's own log, and in each muic_set_path appears zero times. A false positive was caught on the way: searching muic_init returned eight hits per capture which looked like the bootloader running MUIC init, and reading the lines showed all eight are kernel lines, pdic_max77705: max77705_muic_init_regs and max77705_muic_init_detect, in which the search term is only a substring. Enumerating the bootloader's own log confirms it from the other direction: it holds 159 B - microseconds - tag lines whose tags are PM thirty times, usb twelve, DTB eight, Debug Policy four, DDR four, the several UFS tags, INFO, Find DTB for chipinfo, ufs_error_log and Init logs to media, with no MUIC tag at all, and the twelve usb lines are only usb_shared_xbl_dtb_node_init, usb_hs_phy_cfg size, eud_ser_upd, UFS Serial and ldr. The negative is bounded rather than absolute: the retained bootloader log spans 130784 to 762378 microseconds and does not start at zero, so roughly the first 131 ms of bootloader execution is not in this evidence and a MUIC action taken before that or taken without logging is not excluded, which makes the honest statement that the long-standing premise that a candidate may inherit a USB-position mux left by the bootloader is unsupported within the retained window rather than refuted. On EUD the same evidence gives a partial answer, in that the bootloader runs eud_ser_upd twice on every normal boot while usb_eud_is_active never appears so no enable or disable failure was logged, which does not settle whether EUD is enabled in hardware, leaving /sys/module/eud/parameters/enable as the settling read. All inputs are the firmware ZIP already on this host and captures already on this host; nothing was flashed and no bootloader image was modified or written anywhere. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred, and this row creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay or live authority.

2026-08-19T08:54:14Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-role-initiator-1 | H0 | P319_STOCK_ROLE_COMES_FROM_UCSI_OVER_GLINK | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | Correcting the extcon claim left a hole, namely what sets vbus_active on a stock boot if dwc3-msm subscribes to no extcon, and closing it identifies a subsystem this campaign has not been looking at. Four facts eliminate the alternatives. dwc3-msm registers a USB role switch at dwc3-msm-core.c:6091 with .set = dwc3_msm_usb_role_switch_set_role at :6084 which calls dwc3_msm_set_role at :4786. The device-tree default-mode fallback that would otherwise set vbus_active true by itself is guarded at :5564 by if (!mdwc->role_switch && !mdwc->extcon), and because the role switch is registered that fallback does not run. No DT extcon notifier is registered, as the preceding row established from the absence of extcon_register_notifier and extcon_get_edev_by_phandle in the shipped dwc3-msm.ko. And among the shipped vendor_dlkm modules the only importer of usb_role_switch_set_role is dwc3-msm.ko itself, while the entire Samsung Type-C stack under usb/typec/manager, usb/typec/common and usb/typec/maxim contains no usb_role_switch reference at all, so the Samsung stack does not drive the role switch. What remains is UCSI: drivers/usb/typec/ucsi/ucsi.c is a caller of usb_role_switch_set_role, and the core is not a shipped module because ucsi_glink.ko imports ucsi_register, ucsi_create, ucsi_connector_change, ucsi_destroy, ucsi_unregister, ucsi_set_drvdata and ucsi_get_drvdata as undefined symbols and no module in vendor_dlkm defines any of them, which places the UCSI core in the kernel image. The stock role path is therefore PMIC firmware through pmic_glink, qcom_glink and qcom_smd to ucsi_glink.ko, then the built-in UCSI core, then usb_role_switch_set_role, then dwc3_msm_usb_role_switch_set_role and dwc3_msm_set_role, then dwc3_ext_event_notify, sm_work and dwc3_otg_start_peripheral to the pull-up. The retained captures corroborate the ordering without proving the call, since pmic_glink.ko and ucsi_glink.ko load at about 3.42 and 3.45 seconds ahead of pdic_max77705 at 4.09 seconds, while the role-switch call sites log at dev_dbg and are absent from the captures, making this a structural derivation with a load-order corroboration rather than a traced call, and it is recorded as such. Two consequences follow. First it explains a cluster in the P3.17 plan that previously had no explanation: of the 27 genuinely late modules, seven are qcom_glink, qcom_glink_smem, qcom_smd, rproc_qcom_common, pdr_interface, pmic_glink and ucsi_glink, and they are there because they carry the role rather than as incidental platform plumbing. Second it separates two things this campaign has been treating as one, in that the MAX77705 owns the analog D+/D- path through CONTROL1 while UCSI over GLINK owns the role that starts the gadget, so a candidate needs both and the campaign has spent its effort on the first while the second was never mapped. All inputs are the materialized candidate kernel sources, shipped modules read through a read-only loop mount, and captures already on this host. 2026-08-19T09:28:44Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-carrier-bad-body-cause-1 | H0 | P319_BAD_BODY_IS_A_POLICY_REFUSAL_NOT_CORRUPTION | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | With the retained-log carrier established as a working candidate channel, the useful question became why its P3.18 payload decoded as valid then bad-body, and the frame and the decoder are both on this host so the question was answered by running one against the other. The record sits at offset 1649274 in rollback-observer-1.bin immediately after XBL bootloader output and is 192 bytes, a 32-byte header plus two 80-byte slots, with the header decoding cleanly as family S22E1L2 pipe, format version 2, profile E2, run id b9cc424d0d184f5accbce94a844e817d and header_crc_valid true. The campaign's own decode_record returns slot_status valid then bad-body, which is exactly the pair GOAL.md records: slot 0 is valid at generation 46, stage 0x65, outcome PROGRESS, item 37, and slot 1 is bad-body at generation 47, stage 0x66, outcome FAILURE, item 38, detail 0x6010. The decisive detail is which of the three bad-body causes fired, because _decode_slot uses one label for three very different situations. It was not the structural check, since reserved is 0, length is 0 and the padding tail is all zero. It was not the canonical-encoding check, since re-encoding the slot reproduces the raw 80 bytes exactly. And the CRC had already validated before either. Slot 1 is therefore authentic and undamaged, refused by _validate_semantics alone, and the message names the rule at s22plus_fyg8_p294_telemetry_spec.py:417, P2.94 exact detail is outside its declared route; reading that function the path taken is the blanket guard detail >= 0xC00 with the tuple ordinal 46, outcome FAILURE, detail 0x6010 absent from the exact rule set, and 0x6010 is 24592, far above 0xC00. That is a policy refusal of a real datum rather than a corrupted payload. One self-correction is recorded: an earlier run of this decode reported both slots as bad-body because the profile string was guessed as p318 instead of the E2 the header declares, and with the wrong profile even slot 0 fails semantic validation, so the corrected run is the one reported. Credit is also recorded where it is due, because the campaign already knew the slot was bad-body and reads the value deliberately: s22plus_fyg8_p318_historical_eud_index_sweep.py:411-412 carries the same offset 1649274, the same two slot tuples including 24592, the label valid-bad-body-recovered-0x6010, and frozen_decoder_exposed_bad_body_successes of 2, so this is a recorded practice and not an unnoticed integrity gap; what was missing, absent from every report, from GOAL.md and from the sweep itself, is the reason the decoder refused it, which this row names. The consequence reframes the evidence-design work: the retained-log carrier is not lossy and payload integrity is not the problem, since two slots arrived byte-perfect, and the gap is that the emitter can produce failure detail codes the spec's route table does not declare while the decoder then refuses an authentic record rather than surfacing it, so improving candidate evidence means reconciling the emitter's failure-code space with the declared routes, which is an edit to a table on this host, rather than building a new channel. All inputs are run records and repository sources already on this host. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred, and this row creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay or live authority.
2026-08-19T10:04:38Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-detail-route-census-1 | H0 | P319_107_OF_108_FAILURE_CODES_ARE_UNROUTED | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | Naming the rule that refused 0x6010 invited the census of how much of the candidate's failure vocabulary that rule refuses, and the answer is nearly all of it. The emitter vocabulary was collected from the define P-NNN-_DETAIL_ constants across the runtime transforms and is 111 distinct codes, of which three are below 0xC00 and are stage masks rather than failure details, being P282 at 0x0e and P288 at 0x08 and 0x7f, leaving 108 at or above 0xC00 which is the threshold of the blanket guard. Against the two spec generations that expose a route table, s22plus_fyg8_p294_telemetry_spec with 254 declared detail values and s22plus_fyg8_p296_telemetry_spec with 246, exactly one of those 108 is routed, namely P282_DETAIL_CYCLE_TRACE_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE at 0xc01, while the other 107 appear in no declared route at all, so a slot carrying any of them is refused as bad-body wherever it is emitted. 0x6010 is one of the 107, so its bad-body was not an unlucky code: on the validator that ran almost the entire failure vocabulary is unroutable and nearly any failure the candidate could have reported would have been refused identically. Two limits are stated rather than glossed: the P3.13 and P3.14 specs are structured differently and expose no comparable exact_detail_rules so they were not compared and nothing here says whether they route more, and the count is of what the emitter can produce rather than of what any particular candidate build links. Credit is recorded where it belongs, because the meaning of 0x6010 is not new: docs/reports/S22PLUS_FYG8_P318_POSTLIVE_EUD_INDEX_RECOVERY_H0_2026-08-17.md:62 already records that an open, read or close failure returns P307_DETAIL_EUD_CACHE_READ_FAILED, and this row adds the census and the rule rather than the name. 2026-08-19T10:16:51Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-detail-route-design-1 | H0 | P319_UNROUTED_CODES_ARE_LEGACY_DESIGN_ONLY | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The census that 107 of 108 emitter codes have no declared route invites authoring 107 routes, and breaking the number down by the generation that minted each constant shows that would be the wrong move. The split is generational and total: P2.82 contributes 1 code with 0 covered, P2.98 19 with 0, P3.00 13 with 0, P3.01 9 with 0, P3.03 15 with 0, P3.06 6 with 0 and P3.07 10 with 0, while P3.13 contributes 32 with 31 covered and P3.15 3 with 3, so every constant minted from P3.13 onward is covered at 34 of 35 and every constant minted before it, 73 in all, is covered by nothing. The reason is a change of model rather than an omission, because the early specs enumerate an allowlist of exact ordinal, outcome and detail routes with detail constants hand-assigned in blocks from 0x0f60 upward for P2.98 and 0x6001 upward for P3.01 through P3.07, whereas from P3.13 the spec switched to computed families with a_outputs at 0xd00 upward and b_outputs spanning 0x4801 to 0x6fff derived from a matrix rather than listed; 0x6010 sits numerically inside the family B range and is still not a family B value because that range is a computed subset with declared exclusions and 0x6010 is a P3.07 hand-assignment predating the scheme. So bad-body on 0x6010 is neither a bug nor bad luck but a legacy constant validated by a spec generation that no longer mints constants that way. That rules out the obvious fix, because the frozen specs must not be edited: the P3.18 decode and every earlier one were performed against them and changing a route table retroactively would change what past evidence means, which is exactly what adding 107 routes to a frozen allowlist would do. Two changes are available and neither touches a frozen spec. The emitter-side change is that a candidate needing to report one of the 73 legacy conditions should map it into the current family space rather than emit the legacy constant, which is the fix that makes future failures legible and is a change to the runtime transforms rather than to any decoder. The decoder-side change is diagnostic only, since _decode_slot returns bad-body for three unrelated situations being structural violation, semantic refusal and canonical mismatch, and reporting an unrouted detail under its own status would surface legacy codes as legacy rather than as damage without changing whether any slot is accepted. Both are machinery changes and therefore carry a review obligation before they are made, so this unit deliberately stops at the measurement and the design and changes no spec, decoder or runtime. All inputs are repository sources already on this host. 2026-08-19T10:28:13Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-ucsi-candidate-feasibility-1 | H0 | P319_CANDIDATE_CANNOT_REACH_UCSI_ADSP_DRIVER_ABSENT | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | Having identified UCSI over GLINK as the stock role initiator, this unit asks whether the candidate's plan can reproduce it, using the materialized plan the candidate is built from, s22plus_fyg8_p286_e3_plan.h at 70 entries, rather than a reconstruction. The GLINK cluster is present and correctly ordered with qcom_glink at 44, qcom_glink_smem 45, qcom_smd 46, rproc_qcom_common 47, pdr_interface 48, pmic_glink 49 and ucsi_glink at 62, and checked against the ramdisk modules.dep every declared dependency of all seven is both in the plan and loaded before its dependent, giving zero violations. It still cannot work. The device tree states what pmic_glink needs: compatible qcom,pmic-glink with channel PMIC_RTR_ADSP_APPS, subsys-name lpass, protection-domain tms/servreg and msm/adsp/charger_pd, and a qcom,ucsi child, so UCSI is a child of pmic_glink and pmic_glink's channel lives on the ADSP. On stock that channel appears in two steps, with modprobe loading qcom_q6v5_pas at 3.508 seconds and the kernel reporting remoteproc1 3000000.remoteproc-adsp is available at 3.511, while the edge itself 3000000.remoteproc-adsp:glink-edge only comes up at 7.47 seconds after the subsystem has been booted with firmware. Both qcom_q6v5_pas.ko and qcom_q6v5.ko are present in the vendor_boot ramdisk and neither is in the candidate's 70-entry plan, while what the plan does carry is rproc_qcom_common.ko which is the shared helper and not the driver that binds 3000000.remoteproc-adsp. The chain therefore breaks at its first link: no ADSP driver, so no ADSP remoteproc, so no glink-edge, so no PMIC_RTR_ADSP_APPS channel, so pmic_glink has nothing to attach to, so its qcom,ucsi child never appears, so nothing calls usb_role_switch_set_role; a candidate reaching mode_store by writing sysfs is not reproducing the stock path but substituting for a path that cannot run. The methodological point is worth more than the fact, because modules.dep reported zero violations for a cluster that is nonetheless non-functional, which is not a contradiction since modules.dep records symbol dependencies and pmic_glink does not link against qcom_q6v5_pas while what it needs is a device, a GLINK edge that exists only once another driver has booted a remote subsystem; dependency-safe is not the same as functional and this campaign's module planning has been validated against the former. One measurement error was caught before publication and is recorded: an intermediate script reported that the plan contained no rproc, adsp, q6, lpass, servreg or pdr module at all, which contradicted the same session's cluster listing that placed rproc_qcom_common at position 47, and rerunning showed the script's plan list had been empty so all eight of its results were spurious. The scope is stated: this is a static plan and device-tree analysis with stock-log corroboration, not a candidate observation, and it says the plan cannot work rather than that a run was seen failing this way. The open list is updated to ask whether adding the ADSP remoteproc driver is sufficient or whether the msm/adsp/charger_pd protection domain also needs a userspace registrar a candidate cannot provide. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred, and this row creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay or live authority.

2026-08-19T11:00:30Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-bootloader-read-3 | H0 | P319_UEFI_AND_ABL_ARE_FIRMWARE_VOLUMES_ROUTE_REOPENED | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | Two claims this unit published about the bootloader are withdrawn: that uefi.elf, abl.elf and xbl_s.melf are opaque, and that the CONTROL1 value the bootloader writes is unrecoverable from this host. Both were premature, and the error was declaring impossibility without attempting the standard operation, which is the second time in this unit a route was closed without being tried. The measurement that produced the claim was real but insufficient: non-padding entropy of 7.97, 7.99 and 7.32 bits per byte establishes only that a string search cannot see into the files, and entropy does not distinguish encryption from compression, a point the second independent review made and which was corrected in wording without being acted on. Probing the ELF program headers settles it. Both uefi.elf LOAD segment 1 and abl.elf LOAD segment 1 begin with the _FVH signature, and parsing the header yields file-system GUID 78e58c8c-3d8a-4f1c-9935-896185c32dd3 which is EFI_FIRMWARE_FILE_SYSTEM2, header length 72, revision 2, and an FvLength of 0x300000 and 0x252000 matching each LOAD segment size exactly, so these are UEFI firmware volumes in a documented container format. Walking abl.elf's FFS file list returns a real entry of type 0x0b FIRMWARE_VOLUME_IMAGE at 755037 bytes with GUID 9e21fd93-9c72-4c15-8c4b-e77f1db2d792, the LZMA-compressed section GUID, so the volume nests a compressed volume. A third figure was also misleading: xbl_s.melf's first LOAD segment has entropy 6.13, which is not high at all, and the 7.32 previously reported was an average over the whole padded file. The corrected status is therefore that the UEFI and ABL stages are not closed to analysis; unpacking the volumes and decompressing their sections is ordinary work that this unit did not do, so the written CONTROL1 value is not established as unrecoverable but only as not recovered here, and the open list carries it again as untried rather than impossible. The narrower findings that survive are unchanged: the executed MUIC strings appear as plaintext in zero of the 30 extracted images, XblRamdump.elf holds a MUIC path that did not run, and disassembling XblRamdump.elf would therefore disassemble the wrong code. Tests pinning the retired wording were replaced with tests pinning the reopening and asserting the absence of the withdrawn sentence. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred; no bootloader image was modified, and nothing was flashed. This row creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay or live authority.
2026-08-19T11:06:40Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-bootloader-unpack-1 | H0 | P319_TWO_UEFI_VOLUMES_UNPACKED_ABL_COMPOSES_THE_CMDLINE | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The route reopened in the preceding row was attempted rather than left as a plan, and two volumes are now open, decompressed with a minimal reader and no external dependency in the same style as this campaign's sparse and LP readers. abl.elf holds one FFS file of type 0x0b at 755037 bytes containing a single GUID-defined section with GUID ee4e5898-3914-4259-9d6e-dc7bd79403cf, which is EFI_LZMA_CUSTOM_DECOMPRESS, at data offset 24; the payload is a raw LZMA-alone stream beginning 5d 00 00 00 01 and Python's lzma with FORMAT_ALONE expands 754989 bytes to 3592584. imagefv.elf has the same shape with one type-0x0b file of 2707112 bytes expanding to 3166216. One genuine finding comes out of ABL and closes a loop opened earlier in this unit: its strings include common_muic.muic_param_pmic_info=3, common_muic.muic_param_afc_mode=0x%02x and common_muic.muic_param_pdic_info=%d, so ABL is where the kernel command line carrying those module parameters is composed, which is the source this unit inferred when it established that the parameters reach common_muic.ko through the command line and libmodprobe rather than through a modules.options file that does not exist anywhere in the firmware. What ABL does not contain is the MUIC driver: its strings are Error locating the MUIC protocol, Error locating the CCIC protocol, MuicGetDeviceType, MuicGetJigType, MuicGetAdcOrientedDevice, CcicReadAdc and CcicCheckActive, so ABL consumes UEFI protocols that another DXE driver publishes, and none of the executed log strings muic_command_polling, ccic_is_max77705, MUIC Device : Max77705 or max77705_read_adc appears in either decompressed volume. The driver is one nesting level further in: both decompressed blobs are themselves firmware volumes with _FVH at offset 0x30, base 0x8 and FvLength matching the blob size exactly, but their file area does not parse with a standard FFS walker because the first entry's name GUID reads as all 0xFF followed by a repeating f0 00 and f8 pattern that is not an FFS header, which is a Qualcomm-specific layout this unit did not decode. The status is recorded as partial rather than dressed up: the outer containers are open and their format is understood, ABL yielded one real finding, and the MUIC driver has not been reached, leaving the inner file layout as bounded remaining work on files already on this host. The open list item is updated from untried to outer-volumes-unpacked.
