2026-08-19T11:14:45Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-bootloader-unpack-2 | H0 | P319_MUIC_DXE_DRIVER_LOCATED_CONTAINER_CHAIN_DECODED | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The preceding row reported a partial unpack blocked by what it called a Qualcomm-specific layout, and that framing was wrong: both obstacles were mine and the chain is now decoded end to end. The first was a pad-file bug, since an FFS pad file has an all-0xFF name GUID and type 0xf0 and the walker treated that as end-of-list, so the f0 00 and f8 bytes that looked proprietary are a textbook pad file carrying the FV extended header at extoff 96; skipping pad files instead of stopping on them made abl_inner yield four type-9 applications named LinuxLoader, Odin, Cryptest and QuestSOD, and imagefv_inner yield 26 freeform files that are all download-mode jpg artwork. The second was an assumption that the compression was LZMA because the sibling volume used it: uefi.elf yields four files including two of type 0x0b whose GUID-defined sections carry GUID 1d301fe9-be79-4353-91c2-d23bc959ae0c and payloads beginning 1f 8b 08 00, which is gzip, decompressing to 3854344 and 3170312 bytes. The second of those contains every string the captures showed, with muic_command_polling twice and MUIC Device : Max77705, ccic_is_max77705, max77705_read_adc and BC_CTRL1_READ once each, and walking it names the drivers Ccic and Muic at 36946 bytes each with CcicDxe.c among the source-file strings, alongside ccic_init, ccic_command_polling and [CCIC] Max77705 HW i2c init, which confirms ABL is the consumer of the MUIC and CCIC protocols and these are the providers. The container chain is therefore four layers, being uefi.elf to FV to type-0x0b file to GUID-defined gzip section to nested FV to the Muic DXE driver, and nothing in it was encrypted or proprietary. The value written by OP 0x06 is still not read, which needs disassembly of a 36946-byte AArch64 PE, but the route is open end to end and the three successive framings of it as opaque, unrecoverable and Qualcomm-specific were each wrong in turn. This is the third time in this unit that a route was declared closed without the standard operation being attempted. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred; no bootloader image was modified and nothing was flashed. 2026-08-19T11:20:17Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-bootloader-control1-value-1 | H0 | P319_BOOTLOADER_WRITES_COM_OPEN_INHERITANCE_REFUTED | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The value the bootloader writes to CONTROL1 is recovered and it is 0x3f, COM_OPEN. The Muic DXE driver extracts from the innermost volume as a PE32+ EFI boot service driver for ARM64 with three sections and disassembles as pei-aarch64-little with aarch64-linux-gnu-objdump. The function at 0x2268 is the CONTROL1 writer and its tail is the exact sequence the captures show, adding x1 = sp+8 then mov w0 #0x6 and a call, which is opcode 0x06 the CONTROL1 write, then mov w0 #0x5 and a call, which is opcode 0x05 the read-back. Its argument is a path id dispatched through a seven-entry byte table at 0x5881 holding 0b 0d 0f 11 13 00 00 with branch targets computed as 0x22a4 plus entry times four, which decodes the driver's whole CONTROL1 vocabulary as path id 0 writing 0x3f COM_OPEN, id 1 writing 0x09 COM_USB, id 2 writing 0x9b, id 3 writing 0xa4, id 4 writing 0xad, and ids 5 and 6 reading then setting or clearing bit 6. Three callers exist, two passing 0x2 inside a device-type dispatch and a third at 0x21a4 passing wzr, that is path id 0, immediately after a preceding opcode-2 write, which is the initialisation path. The bootloader therefore writes 0x3f and leaves the mux open rather than in the USB position. This closes a question this campaign has carried for months and it closes from two independent directions, since the bootloader's own code writes 0x3f and P3.17's diagnostic read CONTROL1 as 0x3f before writing anything, so a candidate inherits COM_OPEN and the premise that it might inherit a USB-position mux is refuted rather than merely unsupported as an earlier row in this unit concluded. The scope is stated rather than glossed: three callers were found and only one passes 0, and which executes on a given boot was not proven from the binary, with the support being that the captured boots show exactly one OP 0x06 and that the value candidates read back is 0x3f. Reaching this required correcting three successive framings of the route as opaque, unrecoverable and Qualcomm-specific, and the actual obstacles were a pad-file bug in this operator's FFS walker and an assumption that the inner compression was LZMA when it was gzip. All inputs are firmware artifacts already on this host; no bootloader image was modified and nothing was flashed. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred, and this row creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay or live authority.
2026-08-19T11:22:52Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-independent-review-3 | H0 | P319_THIRD_REVIEW_SS_MON_WRONG_AND_UCSI_DOWNGRADED | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | A third independent adversarial review was run with Codex GPT-5.6-Luna at maximum reasoning under codex exec -s read-only and completed, returning nineteen findings against the fc8b94e942 snapshot, of which four are WRONG and fifteen OVERSTATED. Because it reviewed that snapshot it could not see the four commits that followed, in which this operator unpacked the UEFI firmware volumes, located the Muic DXE driver and recovered the CONTROL1 value, so its findings 7 through 10 about XblRamdump inference, the opacity of the images and the strings-only argument are already superseded by work that went further, and its finding 19 about documentation tests pinning prose was already acted on by the evidence-recomputing crosscheck suite. One WRONG is accepted and corrected here. The report claimed the ss_mon function instance is telemetry-only and does exactly one thing, namely call store_usblog_notify, and that is false: usb_reset_notify also sets vbus_current to USB_CURRENT_UNCONFIGURED and calls schedule_work on set_vbus_current_work, which changes the current actually drawn, and it maintains the AOA reset counters and can schedule usb_reset_event_work and raise rst_err_noti, while the instance is a real gadget function that copies descriptors, installs setup and bind callbacks and calls set_usb_enable_state, with allocation also registering a misc device and a GUID attribute. The error was reading the first branch of vbus_session_notify and generalising to both entry points, which is the same narrow-sample shape this operator made four other times in this unit and is recorded as such. What survives is that both entry points are no-ops without an instance and that the module is a mandatory load-time dependency of dwc3-msm; what is withdrawn is that omitting ss_mon.etc costs only log entries, which is now stated as not established. The review's remaining live findings are recorded for action rather than acted on in this row: that the UCSI conclusion is marked PROVED on an elimination that scanned vendor modules only while other built-in-capable callers of usb_role_switch_set_role exist including qcom-pmic-typec, tcpm and usb-conn-gpio; that pmic_glink returns -EPROBE_DEFER while PMIC Glink is down and its RPMSG probe later schedules initialization, so its child never appears is unsupported and the ADSP conclusion is conditional on no later edge producer rather than absolute; that pmic_glink_init_work calls of_platform_populate without requiring a resolved PDR state, so the charger_pd protection domain was treated as a possible hard userspace dependency without checking the gate; and that a retained stock log shows Booting fw image adsp.mdt before the GLINK edge and the remote processor is now up after it, so the edge follows firmware boot initiation rather than completion. Those four bear directly on the UCSI feasibility row and reduce it from a proof to a conditional, which is recorded here and left for the next unit to act on. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred, and this row creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay or live authority.
2026-08-19T11:29:18Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-ucsi-candidate-feasibility-2 | H0 | P319_UCSI_CONCLUSION_DOWNGRADED_TO_CONDITIONAL | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The third review's four objections to the UCSI feasibility conclusion were each verified at source and all four are correct, so that conclusion is rewritten: it survives, but its mechanism, wording and status all change and the earlier row that marked the absolute form PROVED overreached. Wrong as written was the description of a permanent block, since pmic_glink client registration returns -EPROBE_DEFER while the device is down at pmic_glink.c:311-312 and pmic_glink_rpmsg_probe sets state to 1 and schedules initialisation whenever the channel later appears at :479-494, so a late bring-up recovers. Also wrong was this unit's speculation that the msm/adsp/charger_pd protection domain might need a userspace registrar, because pmic_glink_init_work calls of_platform_populate guarded only by child_probed while the PDR check above it decides merely whether to notify clients, at :545-566. What actually supports the conclusion is narrower and was not the reason originally given: schedule_work on init_work occurs exactly once in the driver, at pmic_glink.c:494 inside the rpmsg probe, and the driver states it twice in comments at :158 and :186 that pmic_glink_init_work will be run only after rpmsg, so no rpmsg edge means no of_platform_populate and no qcom,ucsi child, not because anything is blocked but because the only thing that creates the children is never scheduled. The elimination of other role-switch callers holds on this device: the review correctly noted that qcom-pmic-typec, tcpm and usb-conn-gpio also call usb_role_switch_set_role, and none of them can run here because no qcom-pmic-typec, tcpm, usb-conn-gpio, hd3ss3220 or tps6598x module is shipped in vendor_dlkm or the ramdisk and none of qcom,pmic-typec, usb-c-connector, ti,hd3ss3220 or ti,tps6598x appears in the vendor_boot DTB, so built-in or not a driver with no device tree node cannot bind. The log ordering was overstated: the retained baseline capture shows powering up 3000000.remoteproc-adsp and Booting fw image adsp.mdt size 5104 at 6.897 seconds with the GLINK edge 86 milliseconds later at 6.983, so the edge follows boot initiation rather than completion; the same lines also show the ADSP powerup running on a kworker while the CDSP's is driven by init.qti.write. from userspace, which means the ADSP side does not need userspace to start it and strengthens rather than weakens the point that the missing piece is the driver. The corrected status is conditional rather than proved: without qcom_q6v5_pas there is no ADSP remoteproc and therefore no PMIC_RTR_ADSP_APPS rpmsg edge, so the UCSI child is never created and nothing calls usb_role_switch_set_role, while whether some other producer could create that rpmsg channel without the Q6 driver is not established either way. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred, and this row creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay or live authority.
2026-08-19T11:32:03Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-bootloader-control1-value-2 | H0 | P319_EXECUTED_PATH_PINNED_BY_A_MISSING_OPCODE | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The COM_OPEN finding was recorded with a stated limit, that three callers of the CONTROL1 writer exist and only one passes path id 0 while which one executes on a given boot was not proven from the binary. Disassembling the enclosing function removes that limit. The caller at 0x21a4 sits inside muic_init, identified by the string at 0x5abf reading muic_init: Error locating the CCIC protocol, and the function issues opcode 0x01 as a read at 0x2164, prints the format string at 0x5000 plus 0xaec which is [MUIC] BC_CTRL1_READ : 0x%04x, then tests bit 0 of the value with tbnz at 0x2188 and branches past the following opcode 0x02 write when that bit is already set, and finally loads wzr into w0 and calls the writer, which is MuicSetPath with path id 0 and therefore an opcode 0x06 write of 0x3f followed by an opcode 0x05 read-back. The captured boot log is the same sequence including what is missing from it: OP 0x01 then [MUIC] BC_CTRL1_READ : 0x00C5, then no OP 0x02 at all, then OP 0x06 and OP 0x05. The value 0xC5 has bit 0 set, so the tbnz takes the branch and the opcode-2 write is skipped, which is exactly why no OP 0x02 appears in the log. An absent instruction predicted by a value printed two lines earlier is not a coincidence, so the executed path is pinned rather than inferred, and on the captured boots the bootloader ran MuicSetPath(0) and wrote 0x3f, COM_OPEN. The other two callers pass 0x2, which writes 0x9b, and they sit inside a device-type dispatch, so a later attach event can move the mux while the initialisation path leaves it open. This upgrades the preceding row from a value the driver can write to the value the captured boots did write, and it is the strongest form the bootloader half of the inheritance question can take from host evidence. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred; no bootloader image was modified and nothing was flashed. 2026-08-19T11:36:25Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-ccic-driver-1 | H0 | P319_BOOTLOADER_PARKS_THE_CONNECTOR_PRIVATE_CCIC_OPCODE | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | Ccic.efi, the sibling of Muic.efi extracted from the same innermost volume, is analysed and its build path names its origin outright as QcomPkg/Drivers/SamsungDxe/CcicDxe with the object Ccic.dll, so it is a Samsung DXE driver rather than a Qualcomm one. The log line the captures carry, max77705_ccic_set_sink: set to 0, resolves to the function at 0x21f8 whose __func__ string sits at 0x5626 and whose format is a colon set to percent d with two exclamation marks; after logging it loads the value into w1, the constant 0x5e into w0, zero into x2 and one into w3 and issues the command, and the captures show ccic_command_polling OP 0x5E with a matching response on the next line, so the command reaches the chip and is answered. Opcode 0x5E does not exist in the kernel: the opcode enum in max77705.h runs from OPCODE_SAMSUNG_READ_MESSAGE at 0x5D straight to OPCODE_SAMSUNG_SHIPMODE_EN at 0x61 and no 0x5e appears anywhere in the Maxim driver or headers, so this is a bootloader-only command that Linux never issues and does not name. Its caller runs inside CCIC init gated on a check at 0x2538 immediately after chip identification, which matches the captured order exactly, being ccic_init then CCIC Max77705 HW i2c init then ccic_is_max77705 returning 0x1A then max77705_ccic_set_sink set to 0 then OP 0x5E then ccic is found with count 0. Placed beside the MUIC result a picture forms that neither half gives alone: on every captured boot the bootloader parks the connector, clearing sink on the CCIC through a private opcode and writing COM_OPEN to the MUIC's CONTROL1, with both halves being deliberate initialisation rather than leftovers and both leaving the connector quiescent rather than in any USB-carrying state. The limit is the same as for the MUIC half, namely that this is what the two retained captures show and what the code does on that path, and what 0x5E means to the chip is not established, only that Linux never sends it. 2026-08-19T11:42:27Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-abl-setpath-1 | H0 | P319_ABL_SETS_COM_USB_PARKING_READING_WITHDRAWN | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The preceding row concluded that on every captured boot the bootloader parks the connector by clearing sink on the CCIC and writing COM_OPEN to the MUIC, and that reading is withdrawn. It was wrong for the fourth time in this unit in the same way, namely a log format that had not been enumerated. The retained captures carry a third bootloader format beyond B - microseconds - tag and brace-microseconds bracket XBL, which is brace-microseconds bracket ABL, at 1179 lines in one capture and 1188 and 786 in two others, and enumerating it produces the rest of the sequence: MuicGetDeviceType 2, MuicGetAdcOrientedDevice 0, MuicGetVbusStatus 1, MuicGetJigType 0, then SetPath: 1, then Samsung USB Driver enumeration start. SetPath: 1 is MuicSetPath with path id 1, which the jump table decoded from the Muic DXE driver maps to 0x09, COM_USB, and USB enumeration begins about ten milliseconds later. All three captures examined show it at 3.2565, 3.2607 and 3.2606 seconds, so this is the ordinary boot path rather than a special case. The corrected sequence, all before the kernel exists, is that XBL muic_init at about 1.68 seconds calls MuicSetPath(0) writing 0x3f COM_OPEN, then ABL at about 3.26 seconds calls SetPath 1 writing 0x09 COM_USB, then ABL starts USB enumeration. The bootloader therefore does not park the connector: it opens the mux early and routes it to USB before handing off, COM_OPEN is an intermediate state rather than the handoff state, and the CCIC set_sink(0) sits in the early phase rather than describing the final one. This leaves a real tension rather than a tidy answer, because if the bootloader hands off at COM_USB then a candidate should inherit COM_USB, yet P3.17's diagnostic read CONTROL1 as 0x3f before writing anything, so something between ABL's SetPath 1 and a candidate's own first read either resets CONTROL1 or takes a different path; which of those is happening is not established here and is now the sharpest open question the bootloader work produced. One bound from the CCIC half survives unchanged, that what opcode 0x5E means to the chip is not established and only that Linux never sends it. This row also supersedes the earlier conclusion that the inheritance premise is refuted, since that rested on the bootloader leaving COM_OPEN. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred; no bootloader image was modified and nothing was flashed. 2026-08-19T11:47:10Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-abl-setpath-2 | H0 | P319_SETPATH_IS_ODIN_AND_DOWNLOAD_MODE_ROUTES_USB | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The tension the preceding row opened is resolved, and resolving it answers a question this campaign has carried from the beginning. That row found SetPath: 1 in the previously unenumerated ABL log format and concluded the bootloader routes the mux to USB before handing off, which contradicted the candidate's observed pre-read of 0x3f. Reading the ABL format completely shows whose lines they are: at 3.256058 the log reads init cc mode flag 0x0 and Launching odin -927639495, then SetPath: 1 at 3.260724 together with SetDdiBootMode Saving bootloader mode: [1] in DDI, then LaunchAppFromGuidedFv odin Success, then Odin Build Info, then Samsung USB Driver enumeration start, then Odin CmdsInit start and EnumeratePartitions for luns 0 through 3. SetPath: 1 is therefore Odin's, issued after Odin is launched and before Odin brings up its USB stack, and all three examined captures show Launching odin once, SetPath: 1, and Saving bootloader mode: [1], so the bootloader tail in every retained capture is a download-mode session rather than a normal boot, which is exactly what this campaign's own flow produces since it captures and then reboots to Download to flash. The two paths therefore differ and always have: both run XBL muic_init which calls MuicSetPath(0) writing 0x3f COM_OPEN, the Download path continues into Odin which calls MuicSetPath(1) writing 0x09 COM_USB and then enumerates, and the normal path continues into LinuxLoader with no SetPath evidenced. A candidate reads 0x3f because its boot is a normal boot, so XBL leaves COM_OPEN and nothing evidenced touches CONTROL1 before the kernel, and P3.17's pre-read and the bootloader's own code agree after all. This answers why Download mode enumerates to a host while a candidate never has: Download mode does not merely happen to work, because Odin explicitly routes the analog path to USB before starting its USB stack, whereas the normal boot path does not and a candidate inherits an open mux. What this does not establish is stated: there is no capture of a normal boot's ABL stage since all three tails are Odin, and LinuxLoader.efi does contain Error MuicSetPath so it can call the same routine, meaning the claim is that no normal-boot SetPath is evidenced rather than that none occurs. The preceding row's withdrawal of the parking reading stands, but its replacement conclusion that the bootloader routes to USB on every boot is itself withdrawn here as an artifact of attributing Odin's lines to the normal path. 2026-08-19T12:00:38Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-control1-vocabulary-1 | H0 | P319_ALL_FIVE_BOOTLOADER_CONTROL1_VALUES_NAMED | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The five CONTROL1 values the bootloader's jump table can write were recovered from the disassembly but only two were named. All five are now named from the kernel's own bitfield definitions rather than from any external source. max77705-muic.h:294-301 defines the layout as NOBCCOMP at bit 7, RCPS at bit 6, COMP2SW at bits 5 to 3 and COMN1SW at bits 2 to 0, and :376-381 defines the switch codes as USB 0x01, AUDIO 0x02, UART 0x03, USB_CP 0x04, UART_CP 0x05 and OPEN 0x07 with MAX77705_MUIC_RCPS_VAL equal to 0. Recomputing each table entry gives path id 0 as 0x3f which is COM_OPEN by (7<<3) or 7, id 1 as 0x09 which is COM_USB by (1<<3) or 1, id 2 as 0x9b which is COM_UART by 0x80 or (3<<3) or 3, id 3 as 0xa4 which is COM_USB_CP by 0x80 or (4<<3) or 4, and id 4 as 0xad which is COM_UART_CP by 0x80 or (5<<3) or 5, with all five arithmetic checks matching exactly, so the bootloader and the kernel share one CONTROL1 encoding. That also settles what the other two callers of the writer do, since they pass 0x2 which is COM_UART, consistent with their position inside a device-type dispatch that queries MuicGetJigType, and it retroactively strengthens the earlier corroboration that the bootloader shares the kernel's opcode numbering. An external search was run for the one question the repository cannot answer, the meaning of CCIC opcode 0x5E, and it returns a bounded negative: the S22+ kernel's opcode enum skips 0x5D straight to 0x61, the same gap appears in the Note 10 d2s and Tab S6 gts6lwifi Samsung trees so the absence is general to Samsung's Linux drivers rather than specific to this device, and the public sibling chip cannot be carried across because the MAX77958 opcode guide uses a different map with SNK PDO commands at 0x3E and 0x3F. Opcode 0x5E is therefore a bootloader-only command with no public documentation, and its effect remains inferred from the calling function's name max77705_ccic_set_sink and its argument of 0 rather than from any specification. Sources consulted are the ianmacd/d2s and ianmacd/gts6lwifi GitHub mirrors of Samsung kernel sources and the Analog Devices MAX77958 customization script and opcode command guide. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred, and this row creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay or live authority.
2026-08-19T12:05:43Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-bootloader-usb-stack-1 | H0 | P319_UCSI_IS_NOT_A_PRECONDITION_FOR_ENUMERATION | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The UEFI volume holding Muic and Ccic holds 84 files in all and among them a complete USB stack, being UsbfnDwc3Dxe, UsbInitDxe, UsbConfigDxe, UsbPwrCtrlDxe, UsbDeviceDxe, UsbBusDxe, UsbMsdDxe, UsbMassStorageDxe and UsbKbDxe, with a further 56 files in the sibling volume including PmicDxe and QcomChargerApp. UsbfnDwc3Dxe at 102400 bytes is a full DWC3 function driver carrying TRB rings, physical endpoints, StartXfer, EndXfer and UpdateXfer commands, a control state machine that handles reset and disconnect, and charger-port detection through PmicSmbchgProtocol ChargerPort. The structurally important part is how the mode is chosen: UsbConfigDxe exposes a core interface whose assert messages name its members as InitCommon, InitDevice, InitHost, Reset, EnableVbus, GetVbusStatus, PollSSPhyTraining and AdvanceSSCmplPattern, with errors including Cannot Get Connection Mode for Core and Cannot simulate host and device at the same time, so the bootloader selects peripheral mode by calling InitDevice on the core directly and there is no role switch, no UCSI, no extcon and no PDIC anywhere in that path, while Odin nonetheless enumerates to a host on this exact hardware every time this campaign flashes. That changes the weight of this unit's UCSI finding rather than contradicting it: UCSI over GLINK is how the stock kernel chooses to initiate the role and is not a precondition for device-mode enumeration on this SoC, with the bootloader as the existence proof, and a candidate writing peripheral to a600000.ssusb/mode is doing the kernel-side equivalent of InitDevice by setting vbus_active and driving the DRD state machine directly, bypassing UCSI exactly as the bootloader bypasses it. So the earlier conclusion that the candidate cannot reach UCSI is true and much less consequential than it first appeared, because the candidate does not need to reach it, and what the bootloader does that a candidate on a normal boot does not is narrower and already identified, namely that Odin routes the analog path with MuicSetPath(1) before bringing the controller up while a normal boot leaves COM_OPEN. The limit is stated: this is read from strings and the interface's own assert messages rather than from disassembling InitDevice, so what the bootloader programs inside the core and whether any of it differs from what the kernel programs is not established here. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred; no bootloader image was modified and nothing was flashed. This row creates no D0, D1, F1, recovery, replay or live authority.
2026-08-19T12:08:48Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-linuxloader-setpath-1 | H0 | P319_LINUXLOADER_LINKS_THE_MUIC_HELPER_BUT_DOES_NOT_REACH_SETPATH | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The claim that the normal boot path issues no SetPath was established from 80 captures and is now corroborated from the code side. LinuxLoader.efi was extracted at 1671168 bytes and disassembled. It contains the MUIC helper strings Error MuicSetPath, space SetPath colon percent d, Error MuicGetVbusStatus and the rest, and the first 100 bytes of that block are byte-identical to the same block in Odin.efi, which is the signature of one shared static helper linked into both applications. The format string space SetPath colon percent d sits at file offset 0xf1195, and no adrp and add pair in the whole disassembly targets it: the four adrp sites reaching page 0xf1000 pair with 0x4de, 0xdae, 0xc and 0x21, none with 0x195. The string is therefore linked in rather than reached, which is what dead library code looks like, so both the log side and the code side agree that the normal path does not call SetPath. The caution recorded earlier was the right one to have, since the presence of Error MuicSetPath proved linkage rather than a call, which is the inverse of the error this unit made five other times by reading presence as execution. The residual limit is stated: a reference could still exist through a literal pool or a data table, and this pass did not enumerate those. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred; no bootloader image was modified and nothing was flashed. 2026-08-19T12:12:34Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-linuxloader-setpath-2 | H0 | P319_LINUXLOADER_DOES_CALL_SETPATH_DEAD_CODE_CLAIM_WITHDRAWN | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The preceding row concluded that LinuxLoader links the MUIC helper but never reaches SetPath, so the string is dead library code, and that conclusion is withdrawn. It was produced by searching the disassembly for the immediate 0x195 after converting the string's decimal offset 987509 to hex incorrectly, since 987509 is 0xf1175 rather than 0xf1195. Searching the correct immediate finds three call sites, and the one at 0x402d0 is a textbook wrapper: the function locates the MUIC protocol through the protocol table, saves its own path argument with mov w20 w0 at 0x4020c, logs Error locating the MUIC protocol from 0xf0de4 when the protocol is missing, passes the argument through with mov w0 w20 at 0x40270, and logs either Error MuicSetPath from 0xf1160 on failure or space SetPath colon percent d from 0xf1175 with the value in w2 on success. LinuxLoader is therefore the ABL core, which is further evidenced by it carrying Launching odin and SetDdiBootMode while Odin.efi carries neither, and it owns the SetPath wrapper, so the SetPath: 1 line belongs to LinuxLoader's download-mode branch issued before Odin is launched rather than to Odin itself as this unit first attributed it. None of that changes the substantive conclusion, which rests on logs rather than on the disassembly: across 80 ABL stages, 77 download-mode boots log SetPath: 1 and the three normal-boot stages log no SetPath at all, so LinuxLoader has the call and does not take it on a normal boot. What is withdrawn is only the code-side dead-library corroboration, and the error is recorded as the sixth instance in this unit of concluding absence from an incomplete search, this one caused by an arithmetic slip that produced a false negative which was then written up as a positive finding. No device, ADB, USB, Odin, transfer, recovery, replay, live authority, A90 or S20+ action occurred; no bootloader image was modified and nothing was flashed. 2026-08-19T12:15:20Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-linuxloader-setpath-3 | H0 | P319_ALL_THREE_SETPATH_CALLERS_ENUMERATED_NONE_PASSES_ZERO | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The withdrawn dead-library corroboration is replaced with a correct one by enumerating the wrapper's callers rather than searching for a string reference. LinuxLoader's MUIC SetPath wrapper begins at 0x401f8 and has exactly three callers, each with its argument visible. The first at 0x1464 follows immediately after the code logs Launching odin from 0xe3304 and passes 0x1, which is COM_USB, and is therefore the download-mode branch. The second at 0x45a24 is guarded by masking bit 0 off a value and comparing the result to 6, and also passes 0x1. The third at 0x45a98 is guarded by loading a flag at offset 1888 and comparing it to 1, and passes 0x6, which is not a path switch at all because path id 6 lands in the Muic driver's read-modify branch at 0x2314 where the code performs and w8 w19 0xffffffbf, clearing RCPS at bit 6 while leaving COMN1SW and COMP2SW untouched. No call site in LinuxLoader passes 0, so LinuxLoader never writes COM_OPEN and XBL's muic_init remains the only writer of that value; both real path switches pass 1 and both sit behind conditions, one explicitly the download-mode branch and the other a mode test. This matches the log evidence exactly rather than merely being consistent with it, since across 80 ABL stages 77 download-mode boots log SetPath: 1 and the three normal-boot stages log none, which is precisely what a code path taken only under those two guards produces. The conclusion that the normal boot leaves the mux open therefore now rests on both the logs and a complete enumeration of the callers. 2026-08-19T12:20:58Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-xblramdump-rdx-1 | H0 | P319_RAMDUMP_ROUTES_MUX_TO_USB_ALL_THREE_BL_MODES_ENUMERATED | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | XblRamdump.elf has no section headers; its first LOAD segment was disassembled as raw AArch64 at --adjust-vma=0xa7d00000. The string block maps exactly (file offset 1073840 = 0x1062b0 = VA 0xa7e262b0 = page 0xa7e26000 + 0x2b0). At 0xa7d14ac4 the enclosing function loads its own name init_device_for_rdx into x19 and passes it as the %s of every log call, then executes mov w0 #0x1 followed by bl 0xa7d30974 = muic_set_path(1) = COM_USB, unconditionally within that function. This completes all three bootloader modes: normal boot leaves XBL's 0x3f COM_OPEN, download calls MuicSetPath(1), ramdump calls muic_set_path(1). Every mode that talks to a host routes the mux explicitly and only the mode that hands off to Linux does not.

2026-08-19T12:32:55Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-rdx-bringup-thunks-1 | H0 | P319_RDX_BRINGUP_IS_FIVE_CALLS_MUIC_FAILS_SILENTLY | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | init_device_for_rdx was read whole rather than for its one muic_set_path call. It is a branchless sequence of five: muic_init, muic_init_hv_control, muic_set_path(1), ccic_init, ccic_set_sink(1), across two drivers (MUIC near 0xa7d30 and CCIC near 0xa7d96, the latter being the block the kernel drives as pdic_max77705). All five are UEFI protocol thunks over a ready flag and an interface pointer, not implementations: MUIC flag 0xa7f34000+476 iface 0xa7fd9000+3488 slots +0 probe +8 init +16 set_path +24 init_hv_control; CCIC flag 0xa7f7e000+1976 iface 0xa7fad000+1896 slots +0 poll +24 set_sink. muic_init accepts probe bytes 0x1a or 0x10 and retries at most ten times; ccic_init accepts any byte except 0xff with the same budget; the routine called between retries is a single ret. Failure reporting is asymmetric: the MUIC thunks execute a bare ret and return nothing when the flag is clear, while ccic_set_sink returns -2 for a clear flag and -3 for a null vtable slot, which the caller logs as ccic_set_sink finish %d. The caller emits the muic_set_path to USB line before the call and unconditionally, so that string proves the sequence was entered and not that the mux was switched. The register writes behind Muic.efi +24 and Ccic.efi +24 remain unread and Ccic.efi has not been opened.

2026-08-19T13:00:08Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-download-vs-rdx-1 | H0 | P319_ONLY_SETPATH_SEPARATES_DOWNLOAD_FROM_NORMAL_SINK_IS_NOT_A_PRECONDITION | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | The question whether LinuxLoaders download branch repeats the five-call RDX sequence was answered empirically from the closed capture manifest rather than statically. Every MUIC and CCIC bring-up line appears in all 103 ABL-bearing captures, 62 download and 41 normal alike, and every one is tagged XBL: muic_init, ccic_init, both Max77705 HW i2c init lines, BC_CTRL1_READ, ccic_is_max77705, and both command_polling lines. max77705_ccic_set_sink is logged with the value 0 in all 103 captures and with 1 in none. No muic or ccic bring-up line is tagged ABL on any capture. The ABL stage touches the chip on both classes but only to read it: MuicGetDeviceType, MuicGetJigType, MuicGetVbusStatus, MuicGetAdcOrientedDevice and CcicReadAdc each appear in 62 of 62 and 41 of 41. Across the entire MUIC and CCIC surface exactly one line separates the classes, ABL SetPath, present in 62 of 62 download captures and 0 of 41 normal ones. CONSEQUENCE: the five-call sequence is RDX specific and not the general bring-up, since XBL performs four of the five on every captured boot; init_device_for_rdx adds muic_set_path(1) and ccic_set_sink(1) while the download branch adds muic_set_path(1) alone. CORRECTION: the framing that opcode 0x5E is the one remaining gap in a sequence a candidate must reproduce is withdrawn. Download mode is the one path known to enumerate to a host and it does so with the sink left at the value XBL wrote, so setting the sink is not a precondition for enumeration and 0x5E sits off the critical path. The reading that the bootloader parks the connector is likewise narrowed, since the sink clear is unconditional and occurs identically on the boots that go on to enumerate. The instruction to a candidate reduces to a single action, writing COM_USB to CONTROL1.
2026-08-19T13:05:52Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-dwc3-prtcapdir-1 | H0 | P319_BOOTLOADER_WRITES_PRTCAPDIR_DEVICE_NO_HOST_SITE_EXISTS | HEALTHY | PROVED | 0/0 | Review 4 finding 9 objected that the bootloader USB conclusion inferred InitDevice behaviour from names and assert strings while ledger row 375 marked it PROVED. The register evidence now exists. UsbfnDwc3Dxe.efi is AArch64 PE32+ with .text at RVA 0x1000; its MmioRead32 and MmioWrite32 helpers are identifiable at 0xc018 and 0xc050 since both open with tst x0 #0x3 and branch to an assert on a misaligned address, and the DWC3 offsets 0xc100 0xc110 0xc200 0xc2c0 0xc700 0xc704 0xc708 appear as immediates. At 0x3ba0 the driver reads GCTL at base+0xC110, clears PRTCAPDIR bits 13 to 12 with and 0xffffcfff, sets 0x2000 which is PRTCAPDIR 0b10 device, and writes it back. Two facts make this decisive rather than suggestive: the image contains no orr of 0x1000 or 0x3000 anywhere so no site selects host or OTG and the driver has exactly one role, and the write sits on the straight-line success path of the function entered at 0x37b4 which is reached from the drivers own entry point DriverEntryPoint.c at 0x114c through 0x1454 and again from UsbfnExitBootService at 0x1be0, with the only preceding branches being tbnz w0 31 EFI_ERROR checks rather than a role decision. The other orr 0x2000 in the image at 0xfc94 is inside a format routine and unrelated. LIMIT RETAINED: this was traced upward from the register write to the driver entry, not downward from a slot literally named InitDevice, so the evidence attaches to the Usbfn DWC3 init path rather than to that name; and the surrounding 0xc100 0xc200 0xc700 block writes have not been compared against the kernels.

2026-08-20T08:32:46Z | s22plus-fyg8-p319 | h0-irq-numbers-are-not-stock-properties-1 | H0 | P319_IRQ_OFFSETS_GENERALISE_THE_ABSOLUTE_IRQ_NUMBERS_DO_NOT | HEALTHY | REFUTED | 0/0 | Adversarial review of the MAX77705 IRQ and DT audit landed in 281b8e0f17, verified at source against the 121-capture corpus rather than accepted. The device-tree half stands and is the useful half: the nested offsets vbusdet 22, vbadc 23, chgtyp 26 hold in both captures that print nested numbers, since 346 minus 324 equals 352 minus 330 equals 22 and likewise for 23 and 26. The three absolute interrupt numbers are REFUTED as properties of the stock system. The parent IRQ thread name across the corpus yields at least 24 distinct parent numbers, namely 344 348 350 355 356 358 362 363 364 366 367 368 369 370 371 372 373 374 375 376 377 378 379 and 380, and 367 occurs in only 6 of the 111 captures carrying the thread; the nested base is 324 in one capture and 330 in another, the only two that print it. These are dynamically allocated Linux IRQ numbers that shift between boots, and the audit freezes them as identity constants, which binds it to a single blob and will fail closed on any other capture. The audits thread-name derivation is correct and generalises: irq/367-max77705-irq truncated to TASK_COMM_LEN gives irq/367-max7770. The six-token chain is broader than claimed in one respect and narrower in another. Broader: com_to_usb_ap is followed by exactly one opcode_write 06 09 in 10 of 10 notifier-bearing captures, 17 occurrences, counts equal in every capture, which does not depend on the interrupt.
